2. MMEL 22-4 (DDPG) Preparation - Autothrottle System Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task contains the maintenance procedure that prepares the airplane for flight when the
Autothrottle System is inoperative.
NOTE: Use this task if you want to deactivate the autothrottle computer.
NOTE: This task in only applicable to airplanes with a separate autothrottle computer.
B. References
Reference Title
WDM 22-31-11 Wiring Diagram Manual

4. MMEL 22-5 (DDPG) Preparation - Mach Trim Systems Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task contains the maintenance procedure that prepares the airplane for flight when one or
both of the Mach Trim Systems are inoperative.
B. References
Reference Title
22-11-00-740-806 BITE Library Test (P/B 501)
24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)
29-11-00-860-801 Hydraulic System A or B Pressurization (P/B 201)
29-11-00-860-805 Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
210 Subzone - Control Compartment - Body Station 178.00 to Body Station 259.50
22-00-00
D. Single Mach Trim System Deactivation
SUBTASK 22-00-00-860-003
(1) Do these steps to make sure that the remaining Mach Trim System and MACH TRIM FAIL light
operate normally:
(a) If channel A fails, open these circuit breakers and install safety locks:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-1
Row Col Number Name
C 4 C00456 AFCS SYS A MACH TRIM AC
D 4 C00457 AFCS SYS A MACH TRIM DC
(b) If channel B fails, open these circuit breakers and install safety locks:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 2 C01064 AFCS SYS B MACH TRIM DC
C 1 C01037 AFCS SYS B MACH TRIM AC
(c) Make sure that electrical power is supplied (TASK 24-22-00-860-811).
(d) If channel A fails, do the DFCS library test No. 33, Mach Trim, for channel B, do this task:
BITE Library Test, TASK 22-11-00-740-806.
1) Make sure that the test passes.
(e) If channel B fails, do the DFCS library test No. 33, Mach Trim, for channel A, do this task:
BITE Library Test, TASK 22-11-00-740-806.
1) Make sure that the test passes.
E. Dual Mach Trim System Deactivation
SUBTASK 22-00-00-860-001
(1) Do these steps to deactivate the mach trim systems:
WARNING
KEEP PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE FLIGHT CONTROL
SURFACES. THE AILERONS, THE ELEVATORS, THE RUDDER, THE
FLAPS, THE SLATS, THE SPOILERS, THE STABILIZER AND THE NOSE
GEAR CAN MOVE SUDDENLY WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC
POWER. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(a) Supply hydraulic power to hydraulic systems A and B, do this task: Hydraulic System A or
B Pressurization, TASK 29-11-00-860-801.
(b) Make sure that the flaps are set to 0.
(c) Put the stabilizer to 4 units of trim.
(d) Move the control column slightly to put the elevators in the detent position.
(e) Make sure that the elevator trailing edge is aligned with the elevator index mark on the
tailcone at 0.00 ±0.06 in. (0.0 ±1.5 mm).
NOTE: If the elevator trailing edge is out of alignment, a Mach Trim Actuator and Mach
Trim System are required to position it. An actuator or accessory box that does
not operate must be replaced.
NOTE: If the elevator trailing edge is not aligned with the index mark on the fuselage,
dispatch with both Mach Trim Systems inoperative is not allowed.
22-00-00
(f) Open these circuit breakers and install safety locks:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-1
Row Col Number Name
C 4 C00456 AFCS SYS A MACH TRIM AC
D 4 C00457 AFCS SYS A MACH TRIM DC
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 2 C01064 AFCS SYS B MACH TRIM DC
C 1 C01037 AFCS SYS B MACH TRIM AC
(g) Remove hydraulic power from hydraulic systems A and B, do this task: Hydraulic System
A or B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805.

5. MMEL 22-5 (DDPG) Restoration - Mach Trim Systems Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the Mach Trim
Systems inoperative.
B. References
Reference Title
22-11-24 P/B 401 MACH TRIM ACTUATOR - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION
22-11-33-000-801 Flight Control Computer Removal (P/B 401)
22-11-33-400-801 Flight Control Computer Installation (P/B 401)
22-11-37 P/B 401 INTEGRATED FLIGHT SYSTEM ACCESSORY UNIT -
REMOVAL/INSTALLATION
WDM 22-18-11 Wiring Diagram Manual
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
210 Subzone - Control Compartment - Body Station 178.00 to Body Station 259.50
D. Mach Trim System Test
SUBTASK 22-00-00-860-002
(1) Remove the safety locks and close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-1
Row Col Number Name
C 4 C00456 AFCS SYS A MACH TRIM AC
D 4 C00457 AFCS SYS A MACH TRIM DC
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2
Row Col Number Name
B 2 C01064 AFCS SYS B MACH TRIM DC
C 1 C01037 AFCS SYS B MACH TRIM AC
SUBTASK 22-00-00-740-001
(2) Do these steps to do a test of the mach trim system:
22-00-00
(a) From the INIT/REF INDEX screen on the CDU, make these selections:
1) MAINT>
2) <DFCS
3) <FAULT REVIEW
4) <CURRENT STATUS
5) Do steps as required on CDU screen.
6) <CONTINUE
(b) If the CURRENT STATUS test fails, correct the fault shown on the CDU.
(c) If the CURRENT STATUS test passes, continue.
(d) Make these selections on the CDU:
1) <CHANNEL A AND B
2) <MACH TRIM SYSTEM
3) Do steps as required on CDU screen.
4) <CONTINUE
(e) If the surface test passes, then there was an intermittent fault.
(f) If the surface test fails, then make this selection.
1) <TEST RESULTS
(g) Replace the mach trim actuator (MACH TRIM ACTUATOR - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION,
PAGEBLOCK 22-11-24/401).
(h) Select RERUN on the CDU.
(i) If the test passes, you corrected the fault.
(j) If the test fails, continue.
(k) Replace the integrated flight accessory unit(INTEGRATED FLIGHT SYSTEM
ACCESSORY UNIT - REMOVAL/INSTALLATION, PAGEBLOCK 22-11-37/401).
(l) Select RERUN on the CDU.
(m) If the test passes, you corrected the fault.
(n) If the test fails, continue.
(o) Disconnect connector D545 from the mach trim actuator.
(p) For a CHANNEL A problem, do these steps:
1) Remove the FCC-A (M1875). To remove it, do this task: Flight Control Computer
Removal, TASK 22-11-33-000-801.
2) Do these steps to check the wiring of connector D10135C at the E1-1 shelf and
connector D545 in the tailcone (WDM 22-18-11):
a) Examine the connectors for contamination, damage, and bent or pushed back
pins.
b) Do a continuity check from pin to pin. Check for short circuits between the
pins, and from each pin to structure ground.
Table 905/22-00-00-993-809
D10135C D545
pin E4 pin 26
22-00-00
Table 905/22-00-00-993-809 (Continued)
D10135C D545
pin D4 pin 25
(q) For a CHANNEL B problem, do these steps:
1) Remove the FCC-B (M1876). To remove it, do this task: Flight Control Computer
Removal, TASK 22-11-33-000-801.
2) Do these steps to check the wiring of connector D10137C at the E1-1 shelf and
connector D545 in the tailcone (WDM 22-18-11):
a) Examine the connectors for contamination, damage, and bent or pushed back
pins.
b) Do a continuity check from pin to pin. Check for short circuits between the
pins, and from each pin to structure ground.
Table 906/22-00-00-993-810
D10137C D545
pin E4 pin 26
pin D4 pin 25
(r) If you find a problem with the wiring, then do this step:
1) Repair the wiring.
(s) Re-connect D545.
(t) Re-install the FCC, M1875 or M1876. To install it, do this task: Flight Control Computer
Installation, TASK 22-11-33-400-801.
(u) Select RERUN on the CDU.
(v) If the test passes, you corrected the fault.

22-11-00-700-801Land Verify TestPDF p. 36

2. Land Verify Test
A. General
(1) The Land Verify Test does a check on the interfaces between the Digital Flight Control System
(DFCS) and its Line Replaceable Unit (LRU).
(2) The Land Verify Test contains a quick test (autotest), an interactive test and a surface test.
(a) The sequence of the Land Verify Test is as follows:
1) Autotests
2) Interactive Tests
3) Surface Tests
(b) The autotests are automatic.
(c) The interactive tests are not automatic. You must do the instructions that show on the
Control Display Unit (CDU) display to complete the interactive tests.
(d) The surface tests are not automatic and hydraulic power is necessary for the surface
tests. You must do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete the surface
tests.
(3) Autotests
Table 201/22-11-00-993-801
Test Name Group No./ Test No.
ADIRU 49.01
Autothrottle 49.04
DME 49.05
LRRA 49.07
LNAV 49.11*[1]
MCP INTERF 49.16
FMC 49.18
Analog Sensors 49.21
SMYDC 49.22
VOR 49.23
MMR/ILS 49.24
CDS 49.25
MCP Display/Selftest 49.57
Discrete Input 49.70
*[1] Library test 49.11 is available for airplanes with Honeywell Flight Control Computer (FCC)s and with these program
pins:
• For Flight Control Computer A (FCC-A): D10135A –A05/G05 both open or D10135A –A05 is open and D10135A
–G05 is ground
• For FCC-A: D10137A –A05/G05 both open or D10137A –A05 is open and D10137A –G05 is ground.
(4) Interactive Tests

E. Land Verify Test
SUBTASK 22-11-00-740-045
(1) Push the INIT REF key on the CDU keyboard.
(a) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
NOTE: If the CONTINUE shows during the BITE test, do the instructions that show on
the CDU display before you push the LSK that is adjacent to CONTINUE. Use
the NEXT PAGE or PREV PAGE key to change the page if it is necessary.
22-11-00
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
3) DFCS
4) LAND VERIFY
(b) Autotest:
1) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display.
NOTE: If the initialization setup is correct, AUTOTEST IN PROGRESS will show on
the CDU display.
2) If AUTOTEST PASSED shows on the CDU display, the test is completed with no
failure.
NOTE: Some autotests can be completed very fast and you cannot see the
AUTOTEST PASSED message. The autotest passed if you do not see the
AUTOTEST FAILED message.
NOTE: When all the autotests are completed, the summary page shows
AUTOTEST PASSED or AUTOTEST FAILED.
3) If AUTOTEST FAILED shows, do the library test(s) that shows on the CDU display.
4) Make a record of the channel (A, B, or A/B), suspect LRUs and the pin numbers that
show on the CDU display.
NOTE: You can find the channel data adjacent to the INPUT/OUTPUT PINS that
shows on the CDU display. Channel A is for the FCC-A and channel B is for
the Flight Control Computer B (FCC-B). If there is no pin number for the
suspect LRU, the channel data does not show.
NOTE: Each suspect LRU is referred to as a maintenance message.
a) If it is necessary to do the test again, do these steps:
b) Push the LSK adjacent to TEST DATA.
c) Push the LSK adjacent to RERUN.
5) Refer to 22-Maintenance Message Index in the FIM to find the fault isolation task for
the applicable maintenance message.
a) Push the LSK adjacent to CONTINUE.
(c) Interactive Test:
NOTE: The interactive tests come immediately after the last autotest is completed.
1) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete all the interactive
tests.
2) If INTERACTIVE TEST PASSED shows on the CDU display, the test is completed
with no failure.
NOTE: After the last interactive test is completed, the summary page shows TEST
FAILED or ALL TESTS PASSED.
3) If INTERACTIVE TEST FAILED shows on the CDU display, do these steps:
a) Push the LSK adjacent to TEST RESULTS.
22-11-00
4) Make a record of the channel (A, B, or A/B), suspect LRUs and the pin numbers that
show on the CDU display.
NOTE: You can find the channel data adjacent to the INPUT/OUTPUT PINS that
shows on the CDU display. Channel A is for the FCC-A and channel B is for
the FCC-B. If there is no pin number for the suspect LRU, the channel data
does not show.
NOTE: Each suspect LRU is referred to as a maintenance message.
a) If it is necessary to do the test again, do these steps:
b) Push the LSK adjacent to TEST DATA.
c) Push the LSK adjacent to RERUN.
5) Refer to 22-Maintenance Message Index in the FIM to find the fault isolation task for
the applicable maintenance message.
(d) Surface Test:
NOTE: The surface tests come immediately after you complete the last interactive test.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF
ALL CONTROL SURFACES BEFORE YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC
POWER. AILERONS, RUDDERS, ELEVATORS, FLAPS, SPOILERS,
LANDING GEAR, AND THRUST REVERSERS CAN MOVE QUICKLY
WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN CAUSE
INJURIES TO PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT
1) Supply hydraulic power to hydraulic systems A and B. Do this task: Hydraulic
System A or B Pressurization, TASK 29-11-00-860-801.
NOTE: Hydraulic power is necessary for surface test.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF
ALL CONTROL SURFACES BEFORE YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC
POWER. AILERONS, RUDDERS, ELEVATORS, FLAPS, SPOILERS,
LANDING GEAR, AND THRUST REVERSERS CAN MOVE QUICKLY
WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN CAUSE
INJURIES TO PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT
2) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete all the surface tests.
3) If SURFACE TEST PASSED shows on the CDU display, the test is completed with
no failure.
NOTE: After you complete the last surface test, the summary page shows TEST
FAILED or ALL TESTS PASSED.
4) If SURFACE TEST FAILED shows on the CDU display, do these steps:
a) Push the LSK adjacent to TEST RESULTS.

RYR ALL

5) Make a record of the channel (A, B, or A/B), suspect LRUs and the pin numbers that

sure that the autopilot is disengaged.

Row Col Number Name

SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-272

position.
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-273
(4) Remove hydraulic power from hydraulic systems A and B. Do this task: Hydraulic System A or
B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805.
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-337
(5) Turn the ADIRU's off on the Nav Mode select panel, by turning the IRS select switches to off.
22-11-00-740-801Digital Flight Control System (DFCS) - Operational TestPDF p. 44
Open original AMM PDF - page 44 ↗ 
2. Digital Flight Control System (DFCS) - Operational Test
A. General
(1) The Digital Flight Control System (DFCS) operational test does a quick check of the
serviceable condition of the DFCS. It uses no external equipment. Electrical power and
hydraulic power are the only power sources that are necessary for this test.
(2) Do the DFCS operational test from the Control Display Unit (CDU). The first test is a check of
the Flight Control Computer (FCC) software part number. Then use the current status quick
test and five additional tests under the CURRENT STATUS item on the DFCS BITE TEST
Fault Review menu.
(3) The CURRENT STATUS item on the DFCS BITE TEST FAULT REVIEW menu contains these
tests:
(a) Current Status Quick Test (Group 10)
(b) Additional Tests:
1) Autopilot (Group 11)
2) Flight Director (Group 12)
3) Mach Trim System (Group 13)
4) Speed Trim System (Group 14)
22-11-00
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020
5) Stab/Speed Trim System (Group 14)
RYR ALL
6) Altitude Alert (Group 15)
(c) The current status quick test comes before the additional tests. In each additional test, if
there are interactive and surface tests, then the interactive tests come before the surface
tests. The sequence of the Current Status test is as follows:
1) Current Status Quick Test
2) Additional test
a) Interactive tests
b) Surface tests
(4) The current status quick test contains a number of library tests.
NOTE: These library tests are automatic and they are referred to as autotests.
(5) There are five additional tests.
NOTE: Each additional test contains a number of library tests. These library tests are
interactive and/or surface tests.
(a) The interactive tests are not automatic.
NOTE: You must do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete the
interactive tests.
(b) The surface tests are not automatic and hydraulic power is necessary for the surface
tests.
NOTE: You must do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete the
surface tests.

SUBTASK 22-11-00-420-001
(1) Do these steps to test the software part number:
(a) If you are not at one of the DFCS BITE TEST displays, do these steps:
1) Push the INIT REF key on the CDU keyboard.

NOTE: This makes the INIT/REF INDEX show.
3) Push the LSK adjacent to MAINT .
(b) From the MAINT BITE INDEX, push the LSK adjacent to DFCS.
NOTE: The DFCS will do a self-test.
22-11-00

CONFIGURATION.

1) Make sure that the software part number for Flight Control Computer A (FCC-A) is
correct.
NOTE: The software part number shows below FCC Operational Program Software
(OPS).

1) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display.
NOTE: If the initialization setup is correct, AUTOTEST IN PROGRESS will show on
the CDU display.
2) If AUTOTEST PASSED shows on the CDU display, the test is completed with no
failure.
NOTE: Some autotests can be completed very fast and you cannot see the
AUTOTEST PASSED message. The autotest passed if the AUTOTEST
FAILED message is not seen.
NOTE: When all the autotests are completed, the summary page shows
AUTOTEST PASSED or AUTOTEST FAILED.

22-11-00

the FCC-B. If there is no pin number for the suspect LRU, the channel data
does not show.

5. BITE Library Test
A. General
(1) The BITE library Test does a check on the interfaces between the Digital Flight Control System
(DFCS) and its Line Replaceable Unit (LRU)s.
(2) The BITE Library Test contains three types of tests. The quick test (autotest), interactive test
and surface test. The autotest comes before the interactive test, and the interactive test comes
before the surface test.
(a) The sequence of the BITE Library Test is as follows:
1) Autotests
2) Interactive Tests
3) Surface Tests
(b) The autotests are automatic.
(c) The interactive tests are not automatic. You must do the instructions that show on the
Control Display Unit (CDU) display to complete the interactive tests.
(d) The surface tests are not automatic and hydraulic power is necessary for the surface
tests. You must do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete the surface
tests.
22-11-00
(3) Autotests
Table 510/22-11-00-993-815

FMC 60.18

SMYDC 60.22
VOR 60.23
MMR/ILS 60.24
CDS 60.25
MCP Display/Selftest 60.57
Discrete Input 60.70
*[1] Library test 60.11 is available for airplanes with Honeywell Flight Control Computer (FCC)s and with these program
pins:
• For Flight Control Computer A (FCC-A): D10135A –A05/G05 both open or D10135A –A05 is open and D10135A
–G05 is ground
• For FCC-A: D10137A –A05/G05 both open or D10137A –A05 is open and D10137A –G05 is ground.

SUBTASK 22-11-00-740-018
(1) Push the INIT REF key on the CDU keyboard.

1) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display.

the CDU display.
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020; AIRPLANES WITH HONEYWELL FCC HARDWARE INSTALLED
NOTE: If the test of the library 60.30 (30 ELEVATOR) shows “TEST FAILED” at
step S053, but the fail values on the CDU are between the expanded limits
(-2.88, -2.10) and the remainder of the test passes, then the full test is a
TEST PASS.
NOTE: If the system continues to fail with expanded limits, it is necessary to check
the friction in the elevator cable system, including all pulleys. Also it is
necessary to check the spring force of the springs within the feel of
centering (TASK 27-31-64-400-802).
RYR ALL
22-11-00
2) If AUTOTEST PASSED shows on the CDU display, the test is completed with no
failure.
NOTE: Some autotests can be completed very fast and you cannot see the
AUTOTEST PASSED message. The autotest passed if you do not see the
AUTOTEST FAILED message.
NOTE: When all the autotests are completed, the summary page shows
AUTOTEST PASSED or AUTOTEST FAILED.
3) If AUTOTEST FAILED shows, do the library test(s) that shows on the CDU display.
4) Make a record of the channel (A, B, or A/B), suspect LRUs and the pin numbers that
show on the CDU display.
NOTE: You can find the channel data adjacent to the INPUT/OUTPUT PINS that
shows on the CDU display. Channel A is for the FCC-A and channel B is for
the Flight Control Computer B (FCC-B). If there is no pin number for the
suspect LRU, the channel data does not show.
NOTE: Each suspect LRU is referred to as a maintenance message.
a) If it is necessary to do the test again, do these steps:
b) Push the LSK adjacent to TEST DATA.
c) Push the LSK adjacent to RERUN.

the applicable maintenance message.

NOTE: The interactive tests come immediately after you complete the last autotest.
1) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete all the interactive
tests.
NOTE: If the airplane is equipped with a Rockwell Collins MCP (mode control
panel), only 7 segments will be displayed during the DFCS BITE Library
Test 57 and the check can pass. If the airplane is equipped with a
Honeywell MCP and only 7 segments are displayed during the DFCS BITE
Library Test 57, the MCP should be replaced.
NOTE: The A/P disengage aural warning may sound during DFCS BITE Library
Test 58. This warning does not affect performance and test result. Push the
captain or first officer autopilot disengage switch to clear the A/P disengage
aural warning after Library Test 58 is completed.
2) If INTERACTIVE TEST PASSED shows on the CDU display, the test is completed
with no failure.
NOTE: After you complete the last interactive test, the summary page shows TEST
FAILED or ALL TESTS PASSED.
3) If INTERACTIVE TEST FAILED shows on the CDU display, do these steps:

"WHEEL SPIN J10".

position.

"WHEEL SPIN J10".

instructions that show on the CDU display.

(c) Make sure that the B dimension is 39.89 ±0.01 in. (101.32 ±0.03 cm).

22-11-00
SUBTASK 22-11-00-390-002
WARNING
WHEN THE PITOT PROBES HAVE COVERS ON THEM, MAKE SURE THAT A
PERSON ON THE GROUND CAN SEE THE COVERS. ALSO MAKE SURE
YOU ATTACH A TAG TO THE LEFT CONTROL WHEEL IN THE FLIGHT
COMPARTMENT AS A REMINDER THAT THE PITOT PROBES HAVE COVERS
ON THEM. IF THE COVERS ARE NOT REMOVED FROM THE PITOT
PROBES, INCORRECT AIRSPEED-SENSING AND ALTITUDE-SENSING
SIGNALS CAN OCCUR. THIS CAN CAUSE DANGEROUS FLIGHT
CONDITIONS.
(5) At the two pitot probes on the vertical fin, do the following:
(a) Seal the drain hole on each pitot probe.
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE PITOT PROBE HAS NO ADDED WEIGHT ON IT
FROM THE TEST HOSE. THE WEIGHT OF THE TEST HOSE CAN BEND
OR TWIST THE PITOT PROBE OUT OF TOLERANCE.
(b) Connect the regulator, SPL-1742, or equivalent air data model test set, COM-1914, to the
pitot probes.
1) Do this step for the regulator, SPL-1742:
a) Connect the pressure lines from the regulator, SPL-1742, with the pitot test
adapter, COM-1916, to the pitot probes.
2) Do this step for the air data model test set, COM-1914:
a) Use the air data accessory kit, COM-25177, to connect the air data model test
set, COM-1914, to the pitot test adapter, COM-1916.
NOTE: The air data accessory kit, COM-25177, contains the hoses to connect
the air data model test set, COM-1914, and the pitot test adapter,
COM-1916.
SUBTASK 22-11-00-010-008
(6) Open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
311BL Stabilizer Trim Access Door
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-370
(7) Inside the aft unpressurized compartment, below the elevator feel computer, do the following:
(a) Remove the restrictor valves from the bottom of the pitot lines.
(b) If you are using the regulator, SPL-1742, or equivalent, do the following:
1) Connect the pressure gage lines from the regulator, SPL-1742, or equivalent, to the
drain holes of the pitot lines.
(c) If you are using the air data model test set, COM-1914, then do the following:
1) Install plugs over the ends of the pitot lines.
2) Install a highly visible streamer on the opening of the aft unpressurized
compartment.
NOTE: Make sure that you can easily see the streamer from the ground. This is to
remind you to remove the plugs and install the restrictor valves again when
the test is completed.

= 180.16 ±10.00 knots (333.66 ±18.52 km/h).

and sensors.

24. Alpha Vane Rigging Test
A. General
(1) The Alpha Vane Rigging Test does a check on the interfaces between the Digital Flight Control
System (DFCS) and the alpha vane wiring.
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.
Reference Description
SPL-1917 Fixture - Test, Angle of Attack Sensor, ROSEMOUNT AOA's
Part #: J34002-19 Supplier: 81205
Opt Part #: A34012-24 Supplier: 81205
Opt Part #: J34002-18 Supplier: 81205
C. Prepare for the Test
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-288
(1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3
Row Col Number Name
D 18 C00451 LANDING GEAR AURAL WARN
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-198
(2) Set the autopilot stab trim cutout switch, on the control stand, to the CUTOUT position.
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-199
(3) Make sure that the VHF NAV and IRS switches, on the P5 forward overhead panel, are in the
NORMAL positions.
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-200
(4) Set the left and right IRS select switches, on the P5 aft overhead panel, to the ALIGN or NAV
position.
SUBTASK 22-11-00-860-201
(5) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
C 3 C01072 HEATERS ALPHA VANE LEFT
D 3 C01071 HEATERS ALPHA VANE RIGHT

25. Remote Light Sensor Input Test
A. General
(1) The Remote Light Sensor Input Test checks the input to the MCP that will change the LCD
intensity.
(2) The remote light sensors are on the forward part of the glareshield. The sensors face forward.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Procedure
SUBTASK 22-11-00-740-048
(1) Push the INIT REF key on the CDU keyboard.
(2) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
(a) INDEX
(b) MAINT
(c) DFCS
(d) EXTENDED MAINTENANCE
(e) SENSOR VALUES
(f) DIGITAL SIGNALS
(g) MCP
(h) RLS-L.
22-11-00
(3) Point a flashlight at the notch on the front of the left remote light sensor (T507).
(4) Verify that the value of the B channel is greater than 5.00.
(5) Put an opaque lint-free fabric to the left remote light sensor (T507) so the sensor housing is
completely covered.
(6) Verify that the value of the B channel is less than 3.00.
(7) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
(a) PREV MENU
(b) RLS-R.
(8) Point a flashlight at the notch on the front of the right remote light sensor (T508).
(9) Verify that the value of the B channel is greater than 5.00.
(10) Put an opaque lint-free fabric to the right remote light sensor (T508) so the sensor housing is
completely covered.
(11) Verify that the value of the B channel is less than 3.00.
(12) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
(a) EXIT
(b) EXITon TEST COMPLETE screen.

SUBTASK 22-11-11-420-002
(4) Do these steps to install the lower cable [5] of the roll force transducer [1] in the upper spool:
(a) Attach the cable [5] with the cable clamp [11] below the roll force transducer [1].
1) Install the bolt [14], washers [7], spacer [13], nut [6], and plug [12].
2) Make sure that you do not pull the cable [5] tightly.
(b) Wind the cable [5] around the upper spool, approximately 4 turns, in the CCW direction
(as seen from the bottom) from the top to the bottom of the spool.
(c) Turn the control wheel, to the limit, in the direction to tighten the cable [5] around the
spool.
(d) Attach the cable [5] with the cable clamps [11] on the bracket.
1) Install the screw [9], screw [15], washers [10], and plugs [12].
(e) Turn the control wheel clockwise and then counter-clockwise, to the limit, for two or three
times.
1) Make sure that the cable [5] does not prevent the movement.
2) If it is necessary, adjust the cable [5] in the bracket.

(as seen from the bottom) from the top to the bottom of the spool.

times.
1) Make sure that the cable [5] does not prevent the movement.

315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999

(d) Remove the mounting bolt [3], washer [2] and nut [1].
(e) If the voltages on the CDU display (Test 55.18) for the A and B sides are not within ±0.08

NOTE: For minimum error, you must hold the roll force transducer horizontally when you
read the voltage.
1) Make sure that there is no external force applied to the roll force transducer (For
example, if the cable is tightly pulled, then it can cause the voltage to increase).
2) Remove lockwires from the locknuts, fine adjustment nut and the tab washer. To
remove it, do this task: Lockwire, Cotter Pins, and Lockrings - Removal,
TASK 20-10-44-000-801.
3) Loosen the locknuts.

sides are within ±0.08 VAC..
NOTE: The coarse adjustment is approximately 0.016 in. (0.406 mm) for each turn.
22-11-11
(f) Record the voltage.
(g) Turn the coarse adjustment (rod end) until the mounting hole is parallel to the mounting
hole.
(h) Install the mounting bolt [3], washer [2] and nut [1].
(i) Make sure that the voltage is within ±0.01 VAC of the voltage you recorded earlier.
NOTE: Use the fine adjustment nut if necessary.
(j) Push the tab washer against the end of the roll force transducer.
(k) Tighten the locknut on the adjustable sleeve.
(l) Tighten the locknut on the rod end.

Pins, and Lockrings - Installation, TASK 20-10-44-400-801.

and the aileron drum shaft.
1) Install the bolt [4], nut [1], washers [5], spacer [8], clamp [7] and plug [6] if you
remove them earlier.

G. A/P Solenoid and Electrohydraulic Servo Valves Installation
SUBTASK 22-11-12-420-001
(1) Do these steps to install the solenoid valve [1]:
(a) Install the new seal-u-cup [5] on the solenoid valve [1].
22-11-12
1) Make sure that you install the larger diameter of the seal-u-cup [5] against the
solenoid valve [1].
(b) Apply a layer of the hydraulic fluid, D00153, or MCS 352B fluid, D00054, to the two sides
of the gasket plate [4].
(c) Put the new gasket plate [4] in the correct position.
(d) Put the solenoid valve [1] in its position for installation.
(e) Install the screws [2] and washers [3] that attach the solenoid valve [1].
(f) Tighten the screws [2] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) to 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m).
1) Install MS20995C20 lockwire, G50225, on the screws [2] with double twist method.
2) Apply compound, A00226, to the end of the MS20995C20 lockwire, G50225.
SUBTASK 22-11-12-420-002
(2) Do these steps to install the electrohydraulic servo valve [7]:
(a) Install the new seal-u-cup [5] on the electrohydraulic servo valve [7].
1) Make sure that you install the larger diameter of the seal-u-cup [5] against the
electrohydraulic servo valve [7].
(b) Apply a layer of the hydraulic fluid, D00153, or MCS 352B fluid, D00054, to the two sides
of the gasket plate [6].
(c) Put the new gasket plate [6] in the correct position.
(d) Put the electrohydraulic servo valve [7] in its position for installation.
(e) Install the screws [8] and washers [9] that attach the electrohydraulic servo valve [7].
(f) Tighten the screws [8] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) to 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m).
1) Install MS20995C20 lockwire, G50225, on the screws [8] with double twist method.
2) Apply compound, A00226, to the end of the MS20995C20 lockwire, G50225.

I. Post Installation Test
SUBTASK 22-11-12-740-001
(1) Do this Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) test:
(a) Push the INIT REF key on the Control Display Unit (CDU) keyboard.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
NOTE: If the CONTINUE shows during the BITE test, do the instructions that show on
the CDU display before the LSK is pushed that is adjacent to CONTINUE. Use
the NEXT PAGE or PREV PAGE key to change page if it is necessary.
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
3) DFCS
4) EXTENDED MAINTENANCE
5) BITE LIBRARY TEST
6) Make a selection for the applicable channel:
NOTE: A/P aileron actuator A and A/P elevator actuator A have interfaces with the
Flight Control Computer (FCC) A (Channel A). A/P aileron actuator B and
A/P elevator actuator B have interfaces with the FCC B (Channel B).
a) CHANNEL A
b) CHANNEL B
c) CHANNEL A AND B.
7) RUN SELECT LIBRARY TESTS
8) 30 ELEV CONTROL or 31 AIL CONTROL
NOTE: 30 ELEV CONTROL is for the A/P elevator actuators and 31 AIL CONTROL
is for the A/P aileron actuators.
9) EXECUTE.
(c) Do the instructions that show on the CDU to complete the test.
(d) Make sure that the test is completed with no failure or the "TEST FAILED" message does
not show.
SUBTASK 22-11-12-210-001
(2) Visually examine the solenoid valve [1] and electrohydraulic servo valve [7].
(a) Make sure that there is no hydraulic leak.

2. Mach Trim Actuator Removal
(Figure 401)
A. References
Reference Title
27-31-00-800-802 Remove Pressure from the Elevator Hydraulic Systems A and B
(P/B 201)
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.
Reference Description
SPL-1585 Kit - Rigging Pin
Part #: F70207-109 Supplier: 81205
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
315 APU Compartment - Left
316 APU Compartment - Right
D. Access Panels
Number Name/Location
318BR T ailcone Access Door
E. Prepare for the Removal
SUBTASK 22-11-24-860-001
(1) Do this task: Remove Pressure from the Elevator Hydraulic Systems A and B,
TASK 27-31-00-800-802.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-860-002
(2) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-1
Row Col Number Name

(3) To get access to the mach trim actuator [1], open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
318BR T ailcone Access Door
F . Mach Trim Actuator Removal
SUBTASK 22-11-24-820-001
(1) Put rigging pin E-5 from the kit of rigging pin kit, SPL-1585, into its hole in the aft control
quadrant.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-020-001
(2) Disconnect the electrical connector [4] from the mach trim actuator [1].
SUBTASK 22-11-24-020-002
(3) Remove the nut [8], washer [7], nut [6], washer [5], washer [11], and bolt assembly [3], to
disconnect the mach trim actuator [1] from the neutral shift mechanism.
NOTE: Pull up on the aft end of the elevator feel and centering unit to release the load on the
bolt.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-020-005
(4) Remove lockwire from the bolts [2].
SUBTASK 22-11-24-020-003
(5) Remove the bolts [2] that attach the mach trim actuator [1] to the elevator feel and centering
unit.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-020-004
(6) Remove the mach trim actuator [1], washers [9], and bearings [10].

G. Mach Trim Actuator Installation
SUBTASK 22-11-24-820-002
(1) Put rigging pin E-5 from the kit of rigging pin kit, SPL-1585, into its hole in the aft control
quadrant.
22-11-24
SUBTASK 22-11-24-420-001
(2) Make sure that the bearings and washer are in the correct position at the mounting points on
the feel and centering unit.
NOTE: Move the feel and centering unit up or down to put the actuator arm in position. If it is
necessary, remove the rigging pin and push the elevator control surface up, or pull aft
on the control column.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-420-002
(3) Do these steps to install the mach trim actuator [1]:
(a) Install the bearings [10] into the bearing lugs.
(b) Apply sealant, A00247, to the bearings [10] (SOPM 20-50-03).
(c) Put the mach trim actuator [1] with the washers [9] in their positions.
(d) Install the bolts [2] on the sides.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-420-003
(4) Make sure that the crank arm of the neutral shift mechanism can move freely in the clevis of
the mach trim actuator [1] arm.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-420-004
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT YOU TURN THE NEUTRAL SHIFT CRANK IN THE UP
DIRECTION. IF YOU INSTALL THE NEUTRAL SHIFT CRANK INCORRECTLY ,
THE STABILIZER CAN MOVE QUICKLY WHEN YOU ENGAGE THE
AUTOPILOT . THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
(5) Install the bolt assembly [3].
(a) Install the bolt assembly [3] and washer [11] from the left to the right side.
(b) Install the washer [5] and nut [6].
NOTE: This is the larger nut.
(c) Tighten the nut [6] to 85 in-lb (9.6 N·m) - 100 in-lb (11.3 N·m).
CAUTION
DO NOT CHANGE THE TORQUE ON THE LARGE NUT [6] AFTER YOU
TIGHTEN THE SMALL NUT [8]. IF CHANGED, THE TORQUE VALUE WILL
BE INCORRECT .
(d) Install the washer [7] and nut [8].
(e) Tighten the nut [8] to 30 in-lb (3.4 N·m) - 35 in-lb (4.0 N·m).
SUBTASK 22-11-24-420-005
(6) Tighten the bolts [2] on the sides of the mach trim actuator [1] to 20 in-lb (2.3 N·m) - 25 in-lb
(2.8 N·m).
SUBTASK 22-11-24-420-007
(7) Install MS20995NC32 lockwire, G01912, on the bolts [2] with double twist method.
SUBTASK 22-11-24-420-006
(8) Connect the electrical connector [4] to the mach trim actuator [1].
SUBTASK 22-11-24-940-001
(9) Remove the rigging pin E-5 from the aft control quadrant.
22-11-24

G. Autopilot Aileron Actuator Installation
SUBTASK 22-11-25-420-002
(1) Do these steps to install the reducer [10] and union [9]:
(a) Lightly lubricate the new O-rings [11] on the reducer [10] and union [9] with the hydraulic
fluid, D00153.
(b) Install the reducer [10] and O-ring [11] in the pressure port on the autopilot aileron
actuator A [1] or autopilot aileron actuator B [1].
1) Tighten the reducer [10] to 165 in-lb (18.6 N·m) - 175 in-lb (19.8 N·m).
(c) Install the union [9] and O-ring [11] in the port on the autopilot aileron actuator A [1] or
autopilot aileron actuator B [1].
1) Tighten the union [9] to 165 in-lb (18.6 N·m) - 175 in-lb (19.8 N·m).
SUBTASK 22-11-25-420-003
(2) Do these steps to install the fitting [2], swivel nut [7], and union [8]:
NOTE: These items are only on the actuator assy B.
(a) Lightly lubricate the new O-ring [3] on the union [8] with the hydraulic fluid, D00153.
(b) Install the union [8] and O-ring [3] in the pressure port on the autopilot aileron actuator
B [1].
22-11-25
1) Tighten the union [8] to 105 in-lb (11.9 N·m) - 115 in-lb (13 N·m).
(c) Install the fitting [2], retainer [6], and swivel nut [7].
1) Tighten the swivel nut [7] to 105 in-lb (11.9 N·m) - 115 in-lb (13 N·m).
SUBTASK 22-11-25-800-001
(3) Fill the autopilot aileron actuator A [1] or autopilot aileron actuator B [1] with the hydraulic fluid,
D00153.
(a) Seal the reducer [10] and union [9] with plugs.

SHAFT . ITS WEIGHT CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SHAFT .

installation.

1) Make sure that the countersunk side of the washer [21] is against the head of the
bolt.
SUBTASK 22-11-25-200-004
(5) Do these steps to examine the shear rivets on the lever assemblies:
(a) Make sure that there is no unwanted materials in the shear rivet holes.
(b) Do this step to check for deformation of the rivet:
1) Insert a 0.0375 in. (0.952 mm) - 0.0395 in. (1.003 mm) diameter pin in the
inspection hole in each rivet.
(c) Make sure that the pin moves freely while applying force in both directions to the lever
assembly.
(d) If the pin does not move freely, replace the shear rivets (OHM 27-09-05).

NOTE: The position of a tooth not on the spline provides the access.

SUBTASK 22-11-25-200-005
(3) Do these steps to examine the shear rivets on the lever assemblies:

1) Insert a 0.0375 in. (0.952 mm) - 0.0395 in. (1.003 mm) diameter pin in the
inspection hole in each rivet.

SUBTASK 22-11-25-820-001
(5) Do this BITE test:
(a) Push the INIT REF key on the Control Display Unit (CDU) keyboard.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
NOTE: If the CONTINUE shows during the BITE test, then do the instructions that show
on the CDU display before you push the LSK that is adjacent to CONTINUE. Use
the NEXT PAGE or PREV PAGE key to change page if it is necessary.
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
3) DFCS
4) EXTENDED MAINTENANCE
5) RIGGING
6) AILERON
7) AIL RIG.
(c) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display until test 52.04.
NOTE: For detailed data about the AIL RIG test, refer to "Do the Aileron Rigging Check"
in (TASK 22-11-00-820-803).
WARNING
KEEP ALL PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE FLIGHT
CONTROL SURFACES AND THE FLIGHT CONTROL DRIVE
MECHANISMS. THESE COMPONENTS WILL MOVE. THIS CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
1) Do not press the LSK adjacent to “CONTINUE” or exit BITE until the rod
adjustments are complete.
NOTE: If the “CONTINUE” LSK is pushed or BITE is exited the rod assemblies may
move.
2) Test 52.04, AIL LVDT (VAC), Limits: -0.08 to +0.08.
NOTE: Use this CDU display to do the adjustment for the rod assemblies.
3) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are between the limits that show
on the CDU display.
NOTE: Ignore the values check for the AIL POS (VAC) limits on tests 52.01, 52.02,
and 52.03 in this test.
SUBTASK 22-11-25-820-002
(6) Adjust the rod assembly [5].
22-11-25
(a) Adjust the length of the rod assembly [5] until the bolt [1] can go smoothly through the rod
end and the autopilot aileron actuator B lever assembly [8].
(b) Make sure that you can see the bearing thread of the rod end through the inspection hole
at the end of the rod assembly [5].
1) Adjust the rod assembly [5] if it is necessary.
NOTE: The nominal length of the rod assembly is 8.08 in. (205.2 mm).
(c) Tighten the locknut on the rod end.
1) Install lockwire (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
(d) Install the bolt [1], washer [2], washer [3], and nut [4].
1) Make sure that the bolt [1] goes smoothly through the rod end and the lever
assembly.
(e) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are at 0 ±0.08 VAC that show on the
CDU display (Test 52.04).
SUBTASK 22-11-25-820-003
(7) Adjust the rod assembly [7].
(a) Jiggle the captain's control wheel at the neutral position to make sure that the system is
at center.
(b) Adjust the length of the rod assembly [7] until the bolt [1] can go smoothly through the rod
and the autopilot aileron actuator A lever assembly [6].
(c) Make sure that you can see the bearing threads of the rod end through the inspection
hole at the end of the rod assembly [7].
1) Adjust the rod assembly if it is necessary.
NOTE: The nominal length of the rod assembly is 10.35 in. (262.9 mm).
(d) Tighten the locknut on the rod end.
1) Install lockwire (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
(e) Install the bolt [1], washer [2], washer [3], and nut [4].
1) Make sure that the bolt [1] goes smoothly through the rod end and the lever
assembly.
2) Make sure that the installation of the bolt [1] does not cause the ailerons to move
from their rigged position.
NOTE: You can see the movement at the inboard end of the right aileron.
(f) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are at 0 ±0.08 VAC that show on the
CDU display (Test 52.04).
SUBTASK 22-11-25-740-001
WARNING
KEEP ALL PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE FLIGHT CONTROL
SURFACES AND THE FLIGHT CONTROL DRIVE MECHANISMS. THESE
COMPONENTS WILL MOVE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND

1. Autopilot Aileron Actuator Linkage Inspection
(Figure 601)
A. General
(1) This procedure is for an inspection of the worn areas of the autopilot aileron actuator linkage.
To do this check, you must remove the autopilot aileron actuator linkage. Usually you use this
procedure when you do the overhaul of the autopilot aileron actuator linkage. To get access to
the components or to replace the components during the inspection, refer to (Autopilot Aileron
Actuator Removal, TASK 22-11-25-000-801)
B. References
Reference Title
22-11-25-000-801 Autopilot Aileron Actuator Removal (P/B 401)
C. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-1096 Micrometer - Depth, 0-1 inch, Readable to 1/1000 inch
STD-1097 Caliper - Vernier, 0-6 inch, Readable to 1/1000 inch
D. Procedure
SUBTASK 22-11-25-020-003
(1) Remove the parts that are necessary for inspection.
SUBTASK 22-11-25-200-003
(2) Examine the parts:
(a) Use a micrometer (0-1 inch, readable to 1/1000 inch), STD-1096 or a vernier caliper (0 -
6 inch, readable to 1/1000 inch), STD-1097 to measure the parts.
(b) Compare the dimensions you measured with the permitted dimensions shown in
(Figure 601).
(c) Repair or replace the parts that are out of the tolerance.
SUBTASK 22-11-25-420-001
(3) Install the parts.

actuator [1].

actuator [1].

elevator actuator [1].

G. Autopilot Elevator Actuator Installation
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-001
(1) Lightly lubricate the new packings [14] with hydraulic fluid, D00153.
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-013
(2) Install the new packings [14] on the reducer [9] and reducer [10].
SUBTASK 22-11-26-080-001
(3) Remove the protective plugs from the hydraulic ports.
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-011
(4) Install the reducer [9] and reducer [10] in the hydraulic ports on the autopilot elevator
actuator [1].
22-11-26
(a) Tighten the reducer [9] and reducer [10] to 161.5 in-lb (18.2 N·m) - 178.5 in-lb (20.2 N·m).
SUBTASK 22-11-26-800-001
(5) Fill the autopilot elevator actuator [1] with hydraulic fluid, D00153.
SUBTASK 22-11-26-480-003
(6) Install protective plugs on the reducer [9] and reducer [10].
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-002
(7) Hold the autopilot elevator actuator [1] in its position for installation.
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-012
(8) Install the mounting bolts [2] and washers [3].
(a) Make sure that the countersunk side of the washer [3] is against the head of the
mounting bolt [2].
SUBTASK 22-11-26-200-007
(9) Do these steps to examine the shear rivets on the lever assembly [12] or lever assembly [13]:
(a) Make sure that there is no unwanted materials in the shear rivet holes.
(b) Do this step to check for deformation of the rivet:
1) Insert a 0.0375 in. (0.95 mm) – 0.0395 in. (1.0 mm) diameter pin in the inspection
hole in each rivet.
2) Make sure that the pin moves freely while applying force in both directions to the
lever assembly.
3) If the pin does not move freely, replace the shear rivets.
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-003
(10) Install the lever assembly [13] or lever assembly [12]:
(a) Place the lever assembly [13] or lever assembly [12] on the autopilot elevator
actuator [1].
(b) Carefully align the splines.
NOTE: The position of a tooth not on the spline provides the access.
(c) Install the clamp bolt [6], washer [7], and nut [8] to attach the lever assembly [13] or lever
assembly [12] to the autopilot elevator actuator [1].
1) Tighten the nut [8] to 33 in-lb (3.7 N·m) - 35 in-lb (4.0 N·m).
(d) Install the washer [5] and nut [4] on the output shaft of the autopilot elevator actuator [1].
(e) Tighten the nut [4] on the output shaft.
(f) Tighten the clamp bolt [6].
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-004
(11) Connect the hydraulic lines:
(a) Remove the plugs from the hydraulic lines and the plugs on the reducer [9] and
reducer [10].
(b) Connect the hydraulic lines to the hydraulic ports on the autopilot elevator actuator [1].
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-005
(12) Install the hydraulic pressure switch [11] (TASK 22-11-40-400-801).
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-006
(13) Connect the electrical connector for the autopilot elevator actuator [1].

2. Autopilot Elevator Actuator Linkage Inspection
(Figure 601 and Figure 602)
A. General
(1) To do the inspection, it is necessary to remove the autopilot elevator actuator linkages. Usually
this task is used to do the overhaul of the autopilot elevator actuator linkages. To get access to
the components or replace the components during the inspection, do this task: Autopilot
Elevator Actuator - Removal, TASK 22-11-26-000-801.
B. References
Reference Title
22-11-26-000-801 Autopilot Elevator Actuator - Removal (P/B 401)
C. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-1096 Micrometer - Depth, 0-1 inch, Readable to 1/1000 inch
STD-1097 Caliper - Vernier, 0-6 inch, Readable to 1/1000 inch
D. Autopilot Elevator Actuator Linkage Inspection
SUBTASK 22-11-26-200-008
(1) Do a visual check of the both lever assemblies while connected to the autopilot elevator
actuators output shafts (Views D and E, Figure 601).
(a) Manually move each lever assembly.
1) Make sure that the lever assemblies do not have looseness/freeplay while
connected to the autopilot elevator actuator.
NOTE: If looseness/freeplay is present, the lever arm will move sideways, along
the direction of the autopilot elevator output shaft.
(b) If lever assemblies have looseness/freeplay, do the following steps:
1) Tighten the lever assembly nut [9] to 34 ±1 in-lb (3.8 ±0.1 N·m).
a) Make sure that the looseness/freeplay is no longer present.
2) If the looseness/freeplay is still present, replace the lever assembly.
a) Make sure that the looseness is no longer present.
SUBTASK 22-11-26-020-008
(2) Remove the parts that are necessary for inspection (Figure 601).
SUBTASK 22-11-26-200-003
(3) Examine the removed parts as follows:
(a) Use a micrometer (0-1 inch, readable to 1/1000 inch), STD-1096, or a vernier caliper (0 -
6 inch, readable to 1/1000 inch), STD-1097, to measure the parts.
(b) Compare the dimensions you measured with the permitted dimensions (Figure 602).
22-11-26
(c) Repair or replace the parts that are out of the tolerance.
SUBTASK 22-11-26-420-010
(4) Install the parts (Figure 601).

DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .

E. Spoiler Position Sensor Removal
SUBTASK 22-11-27-020-001
(1) Disconnect the electrical connectors of the spoiler position sensor [1].
(a) Remove the lockwire from the electrical connectors.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-020-002
(2) Do these steps to remove the spoiler position sensor [1]:
(a) Remove the bolt [7], washer [8], and nut [5] in the crank [9].
(b) Remove the bolt [2], washer [3], and nut [5] in the mounting bracket [6].
(c) Remove the spoiler position sensor [1].

2. Spoiler Position Sensor Adjustment
(Figure 501)
A. References
Reference Title

20-10-44-400-801 Lockwire, Cotter Pins, and Lockrings - Installation (P/B 401)

Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
563 Left Wing - Spoiler No. 4
663 Right Wing - Spoiler No. 9
C. Prepare for the Adjustment
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-041
(1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3
Row Col Number Name
D 18 C00451 LANDING GEAR AURAL WARN
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-014
WARNING
KEEP PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE FLIGHT CONTROL
SURFACES. THE AILERONS, THE ELEVATORS, THE RUDDER, THE FLAPS,
THE SLATS, THE SPOILERS, THE STABILIZER AND THE NOSE GEAR CAN
MOVE SUDDENLY WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(2) Supply hydraulic power to hydraulic systems A and B (TASK 29-11-00-860-801).
22-11-27
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-015
(3) Do this task: Extend the Trailing Edge Flaps, TASK 27-51-00-860-803.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-016
(4) Set the speedbrake control lever to the DOWN position.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-017
(5) Set the aileron trim to zero.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-018
(6) Put the pilot's control wheel in the center position.
(a) Jiggle the pilot's control wheel.
(b) Make sure that the pilot's control wheel is in the center position.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-019
(7) Set the autopilot stab trim cutout switch, on the control stand, to the CUTOUT position.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-020
(8) Make sure that the VHF NAV and IRS switches, on the P5 forward overhead panel, are in the
NORMAL positions.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-021
(9) Set the left and right IRS select switches, on the P5 aft overhead panel, to the ALIGN or NAV
position.
D. Procedure
NOTE: Two persons are necessary for this task.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-820-003
(1) Do this BITE test:
(a) Push the INIT REF key on the Control Display Unit (CDU) keyboard.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
NOTE: If the CONTINUE shows during the BITE test, then do the instructions that show
on the CDU display before you push the LSK that is adjacent to CONTINUE. Use
the NEXT PAGE or PREV PAGE key to change page if it is necessary.
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
3) DFCS
4) EXTENDED MAINTENANCE
5) RIGGING
6) AILERON
7) SPOILER.
(c) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display until test 52.23.
NOTE: For detailed data about the SPOILER test, refer to "Do the Spoiler Check" in
(TASK 22-11-00-820-803).
(d) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are between the limits that show on
the CDU display.
1) Test 52.23, SPOILER 4 (VAC), Limits: -0.20 to +0.20
NOTE: Use this CDU display to do the adjustment for the spoiler position sensor
(No. 4).
22-11-27
WARNING
DO NOT PUSH THE LSK WHEN YOU ADJUST THE SPOILER POSITION
SENSOR. THE AILERONS, SPOILERS AND FLIGHT CONTROL
SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK. THIS CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(e) Adjust the spoiler position sensor (No. 4):
1) Loosen the bolt [1] on the mounting bracket until you can turn the spoiler position
sensor.
NOTE: You do not have to remove the bolt, washers, and nut.
2) Turn the spoiler position sensor until you get a value less than 0.10 VAC.
3) Tighten the bolt [1] on the mounting bracket to 35 in-lb (4 N·m).
4) Do this check:
a) Slowly move the speedbrake lever aft and look at the CDU display while the
spoilers start to move up.
b) If the value on the CDU decreases toward zero before it increases when the
speed brake starts to move up, then do these steps:
c) Put the speedbrake lever to the DOWN position.
d) Do the above adjustment for the spoiler position sensor (No. 4) again.
e) If the value on the CDU does not decrease toward zero before it increases
when the speedbrake starts to move up, then continue.
f) Remove the lockwire from the thumbnut on the micro-adjustment rod
(TASK 20-10-44-000-801).
g) Turn the thumbnut on the micro-adjustment rod until you get a smallest
possible value less than 0.06 VAC.
h) Install lockwire on the thumbnut (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
WARNING
DO NOT PUSH THE LSK WHEN YOU ADJUST THE SPOILER
POSITION SENSOR. THE AILERONS, SPOILERS AND FLIGHT
CONTROL SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK.
THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
i) Push the LSK that is adjacent to <CONTINUE.
j) Test 52.24, SPOILER 9 (VAC), Limits: -0.20 to +0.20
NOTE: Use this CDU display to do the adjustment for the spoiler position
sensor (No. 9).
WARNING
DO NOT PUSH THE LSK WHEN YOU ADJUST THE SPOILER POSITION
SENSOR. THE AILERONS, SPOILERS AND FLIGHT CONTROL
SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK. THIS CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(f) Adjust the spoiler position sensor (No. 9):
1) Loosen the bolt [1] on the mounting bracket until you can turn the spoiler position
sensor.
NOTE: You do not have to remove the bolt, washers, and nut.
2) Turn the spoiler position sensor until you get a value less than 0.10 VAC.
22-11-27
3) Tighten the bolt [1] on the mounting bracket to 35 in-lb (4 N·m).
4) Do this check:
a) Slowly move the speedbrake lever aft and look at the CDU display while the
spoilers start to move up.
b) If the value on the CDU decreases toward zero before it increases when the
speed brake starts to move up, then do these steps:
c) Put the speedbrake lever to the DOWN position.
d) Do the above adjustment for the spoiler position sensor (No. 9) again.
e) If the value on the CDU does not decrease toward zero before it increases
when the speed brake starts to move up, then continue.
f) Remove the lockwire from the thumbnut on the micro-adjustment rod
(TASK 20-10-44-000-801).
g) Turn the thumbnut on the micro-adjustment rod until you get a smallest
possible value less than 0.06 VAC.
h) Install lockwire on the thumbnut (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
WARNING
DO NOT PUSH THE LSK WHEN YOU ADJUST THE SPOILER
POSITION SENSOR. THE AILERONS, SPOILERS AND FLIGHT
CONTROL SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK.
THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
5) Push the LSK that is adjacent to <CONTINUE (on the CDU display - Test 52.24).
a) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete the SPOILER
test.
b) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are between the limits that
show on the CDU display.
E. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-022
(1) Push the captain's or first officer's autopilot disengage switch, on the control wheel, to make
sure that the autopilot is disengaged.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-038
(2) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3
Row Col Number Name
D 18 C00451 LANDING GEAR AURAL WARN
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-024
(3) Set the autopilot stab trim cutout switch, on the control stand, to the NORMAL position.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-025
(4) Do this task: Retract the Trailing Edge Flaps, TASK 27-51-00-860-804.
SUBTASK 22-11-27-860-026
(5) Remove hydraulic power from hydraulic systems A and B (TASK 29-11-00-860-805).

E. Procedure
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-037
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF THE
FLIGHT CONTROLS SURFACES. MAKE SURE THAT THE INBOARD FAN
DUCT COWL AND THE INBOARD AND OUTBOARD THRUST REVERSERS
ARE CLOSED. THE FLIGHT CONTROL SURFACES CAN MOVE SUDDENLY
WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:

F . Aileron Position Sensor - Tryout
NOTE: This tryout is to make sure the Aileron Position Sensor is in a zero energy state.
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-040
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF THE
FLIGHT CONTROLS SURFACES. MAKE SURE THAT THE INBOARD FAN
DUCT COWL AND THE INBOARD AND OUTBOARD THRUST REVERSERS
ARE CLOSED. THE FLIGHT CONTROL SURFACES CAN MOVE SUDDENLY

22-11-29
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-005
WARNING
KEEP PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE FLIGHT CONTROL
SURFACES. THE AILERONS, THE ELEVATORS, THE RUDDER, THE FLAPS,
THE SLATS, THE SPOILERS, THE STABILIZER AND THE NOSE GEAR CAN
MOVE SUDDENLY WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .

(7) Make sure that the ailerons and the aileron trim are set to zero.

WARNING
YOU MUST CAREFULLY INSTALL THE GROUND LOCKS IN ALL LANDING
GEAR. AN ACCIDENTAL RETRACTION OF THE LANDING GEAR CAN CAUSE
INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(9) Make sure that you install the landing gear downlock pins (TASK 32-00-01-480-801).
D. Procedure
NOTE: Two persons are necessary for this task.
SUBTASK 22-11-29-820-001
(1) Do this BITE test:
(a) Push the INIT REF key on the CDU keyboard.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
NOTE: If the CONTINUE shows during the BITE test, then do the instructions that show
on the CDU display before you push the LSK that is adjacent to CONTINUE. Use
the NEXT PAGE or PREV PAGE key to change page if it is necessary.
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
3) DFCS
4) EXTENDED MAINTENANCE
5) RIGGING
6) AILERON
7) AIL RIG
8) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display until test 52.01.
NOTE: For detailed data about the AIL RIG test, refer to "Do the Aileron Rigging
Check" in (TASK 22-11-00-820-803).
9) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are between the limits that show
on the CDU display.
a) Test 52.01, AIL POS (VAC), Limits: -0.06 to +0.06
NOTE: Use this CDU display to do the adjustment for the aileron position
sensor.
22-11-29
WARNING
DO NOT SELECT <CONTINUE ON TEST 52.01 OR TEST 51.01
WHEN YOU ADJUST THE SENSOR. THE FLIGHT CONTROL
SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK NEXT TO
<CONTINUE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE
TO EQUIPMENT .
10) Adjust the aileron position sensor:
a) Loosen the bolt [1] on the clamp until you can turn the aileron position sensor.
NOTE: You do not have to remove the bolt [1], washer [2] and nut [3].
b) Turn the aileron position sensor slowly in the clockwise and counter-clockwise
directions until you get a value less than 0.06 VAC.
c) Tighten the bolt [1] on the clamp to 35 in-lb (4 N·m).
d) Remove the lockwire on the thumb nut on the micro-adjustment rod
(TASK 20-10-44-000-801).
e) Turn the thumb nut on the micro-adjustment rod until you get a smallest value
less than 0.03 VAC.
f) Install the lockwire on the thumb nut on the micro-adjustment rod
(TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
WARNING
KEEP PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE AILERON
POSITION SENSOR AND FLIGHT CONTROL SURFACES. THESE
PARTS CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK NEXT TO
<CONTINUE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE
TO EQUIPMENT .
11) Push the LSK that is adjacent to <CONTINUE (on the CDU display - Test 52.01).
12) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete the AIL RIG test.
13) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are between the limits that show
on the CDU display.
E. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-009
(1) Push the captain's or first officer's autopilot disengage switch on the control wheel to make
sure that the autopilot is disengaged.
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-032
(2) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3
Row Col Number Name
D 18 C00451 LANDING GEAR AURAL WARN
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-011
(3) Set the autopilot stab trim cutout switch, on the control stand, to the NORMAL position.
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-012
(4) Remove hydraulic power from hydraulic systems A and B. To remove hydraulic power, do this
task: Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805.
22-11-29
SUBTASK 22-11-29-860-013
(5) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812.

adjustment rod length is 6.70 ±0.02 in. (170.18 ±0.51 mm) and installed correctly.
Adjust the micro-adjustment rod 69-43490 with the knurled thumbnut to obtain a
reading of 0.00 ± .07 VAC.

SUBTASK 22-11-31-820-001
(1) Do this BITE test:
(a) Push the INIT REF key on the CDU keyboard.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
NOTE: If the CONTINUE shows during the BITE test, then do the instructions that show
on the CDU display before you push the LSK that is adjacent to CONTINUE. Use
the NEXT PAGE or PREV PAGE key to change page if it is necessary.
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
3) DFCS
4) EXTENDED MAINTENANCE
5) RIGGING
6) STABILIZER
7) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display until test 54.01.
NOTE: Use the trammel trammel bar, SPL-1677 for measurement of the "B"
dimension that shows on the CDU display (Test 54.01).
NOTE: For detailed data about the STAB RIG test, refer to Stabilizer Rigging Test,
TASK 22-11-00-820-805.
a) Test 54.01, STAB POS (VAC), Limits: -0.07 to +0.07
NOTE: Use this CDU display to do the adjustment for the stabilizer position
sensors.
b) Jiggle the aft quadrant.
c) Make sure that the right elevator trailing edge aligns with the elevator index on
the tail cone at 0 ±0.06 inch.
WARNING
DO NOT PUSH THE LSK THAT IS ADJACENT TO <CONTINUE ON
TEST 54.01 WHEN YOU ADJUST THE STABILIZER POSITION
SENSOR. THE ACTUATORS, ROD ASSEMBLIES AND FLIGHT
CONTROL SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK
THAT IS ADJACENT TO <CONTINUE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO
PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
8) Adjust the stabilizer position sensor A [4]:
22-11-31
a) Loosen the bolt [1] on the bracket until you can turn the stabilizer position
sensor.
NOTE: You do not have to remove the bolt [1], washer [2] and nut [3].
b) Turn the stabilizer position sensor slowly in the clockwise and
counter-clockwise directions until the voltage is less than 0.0 ±0.5 VAC.
c) Tighten the bolt [1] on the bracket to 35 in-lb (4 N·m) .
d) Remove the lockwire on the micro-adjustment nut (TASK 20-10-44-000-801).
e) Turn the micro-adjustment nut until you get a smallest possible voltage that is
between -0.07 and +0.07 VAC.
f) Install the lockwire on the micro-adjustment nut (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
WARNING
DO NOT PUSH THE LSK THAT IS ADJACENT TO <CONTINUE ON
TEST 51.01 WHEN YOU ADJUST THE STABILIZER POSITION
SENSOR. THE ACTUATORS, ROD ASSEMBLIES AND FLIGHT
CONTROL SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK
THAT IS ADJACENT TO <CONTINUE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO
PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
9) Adjust the stabilizer position sensor B [5]:
a) Loosen the bolt [1] on the bracket until you can turn the stabilizer position
sensor.
NOTE: You do not have to remove the bolt [1], washer [2] and nut [3].
b) Turn the stabilizer position sensor slowly in the clockwise and
counter-clockwise directions until the voltage is less than 0.0 ±0.5 VAC.
c) Tighten the bolt [1] on the bracket to 35 in-lb (4 N·m).
d) Remove the lockwire on the micro-adjustment nut (TASK 20-10-44-000-801).
e) Turn the micro-adjustment nut until you get a smallest possible voltage that is
between -0.07 and +0.07 VAC.
f) Install the lockwire on the micro-adjustment nut (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
WARNING
KEEP PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE STABILIZER
POSITION SENSOR. THE ACTUATORS, ROD ASSEMBLIES AND FLIGHT
CONTROL SURFACES CAN MOVE WHEN YOU PUSH THE LSK THAT IS
ADJACENT TO <CONTINUE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS
OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(c) Push the LSK that is adjacent to <CONTINUE (on the CDU display - Test 54.01).
1) Do the instructions that show on the CDU display to complete the STAB RIG test.
2) Make sure that the values for the A side and B side are between the limits that show
on the CDU display.

SUBTASK 22-11-32-020-002
(2) Do these steps to remove the annunciator assembly [1]:
(a) Loosen the two fasteners [3] that attach the clamp to the annunciator assembly [1].
NOTE: The two fasteners used to remove the ASA are the top left and bottom right
corners. Do not loosen the top right and the lower left adjustment screws [2].
These two screws hold the annunciator in its position behind the instrument
panel.
(b) Carefully pull the annunciator assembly [1] out of the instrument panel.
(c) Disconnect the two electrical connectors [4].
(d) Install the dust caps on the electrical connectors [4].

22-11-33-470-820FCC Software Installation from the Flight Compartment with a Portable Data LoaderPDF p. 491
Open original AMM PDF - page 491 ↗ 
4. FCC Software Installation from the Flight Compartment with a Portable Data Loader
A. General
(1) This procedure tells you how to install Operational Program Software (OPS) in the Flight
Control Computer (FCC).
(2) A portable data loader (PDL) is necessary for this procedure. A data loader control panel and a
PDL interface connector are also necessary. There must be an FCC switch position on the
data loader control panel. The data loader control panel is installed above the DATA
TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector on the P61 panel.
(3) A PDL is not a Boeing supplied part. Refer to the PDL supplier for instructions for operation.
PDLs have a disk drive for software installation from disks. Some PDLs have an internal mass
storage device. If the software is stored in the PDL, then disks are not necessary.
(4) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can install software.
(5) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer
are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as
preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders
procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current
capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.
B. References
Reference Title
20-15-11-400-801 On-Airplane Software - Installation (P/B 201)
C. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

I. Software Installation Test from the FMCS CDU
SUBTASK 22-11-33-420-010
(1) Do these steps to test the software installation:
(a) If you are not at one of the DFCS BITE TEST displays, then do these steps:
1) Push the INIT REF function key.
2) If the POS INIT display shows, then push the line select key adjacent to the INDEX
prompt.
NOTE: This makes the INIT/REF INDEX show.
3) Push the line select key adjacent to the MAINT prompt.
(b) From the MAINT BITE INDEX, push the line select key adjacent to the DFCS prompt.
NOTE: The DFCS will do a self-test.
(c) When the self-test is complete, push the line select key adjacent to the IDENTIFICATION
AND CONFIGURATION prompt.
(d) Push the line select key adjacent to the CHANNEL A prompt.
1) Make sure that the software part number for FCC A is correct.
NOTE: The software part number shows below FCC OPS.
NOTE: Refer to the Expendables/Parts table for FCC OPS [1].
(e) Push the line select key adjacent to the PREV MENU prompt.
(f) Push the line select key adjacent to the CHANNEL B prompt.
1) Make sure that the software part number for FCC B is correct.
NOTE: The software part number shows below FCC OPS.
NOTE: Refer to the Expendables/Parts table for FCC OPS [1].
(g) Push the line select key adjacent to the PREV MENU prompt.
(h) Push the line select key adjacent to the PREV MENU prompt.
(i) Push the line select key adjacent to the FAULT REVIEW prompt.
(j) Push the line select key adjacent to the ERASE FAULT HISTORY prompt.
(k) Push the line select key adjacent to the CHANNEL A AND B prompt.
(l) Push the line select key adjacent to the YES prompt.
(m) Push and release the line select key adjacent to the EXIT prompt until you are out of
DFCS BITE.

5. FCC Inflight Faults Memory Download with a Portable Data Loader
A. General
(1) This procedure tells you how to download Flights Faults Memory in the FCC.
(2) A portable data loader (PDL) is necessary for this procedure. A data loader panel and a PDL
interface connector cable are also necessary. There must be an FCC switch position on the
data loader selector panel. The data loader selector switch is installed above the DATA
TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector on the (P61) panel. The PDL interface connector
cable is hooked up to the data transfer unit receptacle connector for this procedure.
(3) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can download the
FCC flight faults memory.
(4) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer
are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as
preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders
procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current
capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.

22-11-33-470-822FCC BITE Test Data Download from the Flight Compartment with a Portable Data LoaderPDF p. 503

6. FCC BITE Test Data Download from the Flight Compartment with a Portable Data Loader
A. General
(1) This procedure tells you how to download Flights Faults Memory in the FCC.
(2) A portable data loader (PDL) is necessary for this procedure. A data loader control panel and a
PDL interface connector are also necessary. There must be an FCC switch position on the
data loader control panel. The data loader control panel is installed above the DATA
TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector on the (P61) panel.
(3) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can install software.
(4) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer
are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as
preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders
procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current
capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

7. FCC Software Installation from the Electrical and Electronics Compartment with a Portable Data
Loader
A. General
(1) This procedure tells you how to install Operational Program Software (OPS) in the Flight
Control Computer (FCC).
(2) A portable data loader (PDL) is necessary for this procedure.
(3) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can install software.
(4) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer
are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as
preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders
procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current
capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.
22-11-33

8. FCC Software Installation using the Cross-load Function
A. General
(1) This procedure tells you how to install Operational Program Software (OPS) from one Flight
Control Computer (FCC) to the other FCC.
(2) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can cross-load
software.

SUBTASK 22-11-33-860-063
(4) Do these steps to install the software into an FCC:
(a) On the FMCS CDU, if you are not at one of the DFCS BITE TEST displays, then do these
steps:
1) Push the INIT REF function key.
2) If the POS INIT display shows, then push the line select key adjacent to the INDEX
prompt.
NOTE: This makes the INIT/REF INDEX show.
3) Push the line select key adjacent to the MAINT prompt.
(b) From the MAINT BITE INDEX, push the line select key adjacent to the DFCS prompt.
NOTE: The DFCS will do a self-test.
(c) When the self-test is complete, push the line select key adjacent to the IDENTIFICATION
AND CONFIGURATION prompt.
(d) Push the line select key adjacent to the CROSSLOAD prompt.
NOTE: The CROSSLOAD prompt only appears if the FCCs have different OPS part
numbers installed. The OPS and OPC software part numbers will be displayed
for both FCC-A and FCC-B.
(e) To load software from FCC-A to FCC-B, push the line select key adjacent to the A TO B
prompt.
(f) To load software from FCC-B to FCC-A, push the line select key adjacent to the B TO A
prompt.
(g) To start the software cross-load, push the line select key adjacent to the YES prompt.
(h) If the software cross-load is successful, the following BITE screen will be displayed:

NOTE: If Flight Control Computer B (FCC-B) was replaced, select CHANNEL B.
8) YES

continue.

22-11-34-100-801DFCS Mode Control Panel CleaningPDF p. 533
Open original AMM PDF - page 533 ↗ 
2. DFCS Mode Control Panel Cleaning
A. General
(1) The mode control panel must not be cleaned with the incorrect liquid. The procedure cautions
must be obeyed to make sure that there is no damage to the mode control panel.
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B00130 Alcohol - Isopropyl TT-I-735
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Procedure
SUBTASK 22-11-34-420-001
(1) Clean the surface of the MCP:
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE OUTLET HOSE OF COMPRESSED AIR HAS AN
AIR IONIZING GUN ATTACHMENT OR AIR IONIZING NOZZLE. YOU CAN
DAMAGE THE MCP IF THE AIR IS NOT IONIZED.
(a) Use the compressed air or a brush (soft and natural bristle) to remove light dirt and dust
from the surface of the MCP .
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY THE ISOPROPYL ALCOHOL IN LARGE QUANTITIES.
THE LIQUID MAY GET INSIDE A COMPONENT OF THE MCP AND
CAUSE DAMAGE.
(b) Use the cotton swab with a small quantity of alcohol, B00130 to remove heavy oil and
grease from the surface of the MCP .
CAUTION
DO NOT USE LIQUID CLEANING AGENTS TO CLEAN THE LIQUID
CRYSTAL DISPLAY (LCD). THE LIQUID CLEANING AGENTS CAN CAUSE
MOISTURE CONTAMINATION IN THE LCD MODULES.
(c) Use the lens tissue to remove dust, dirt, oil, and grease from the LCD displays and
edgelight panel.

2. Mode Control Panel Installation Test
A. General

B. References

SUBTASK 22-11-34-860-004
(1) On the captain's main instrument panel (P1), turn the control knob of the AFDS FLOOD lights
approximately to the middle position.
SUBTASK 22-11-34-740-001
(2) Do the Line Replaceable Unit (LRU) replacement test for the Digital Flight Control System
(DFCS) Mode Control Panel (MCP):
(a) Prepare for the LRU replacement test:
1) Make sure that the BAT switch on the Pilots' Forward Overhead Panel (P5) is in the
ON position.
2) Set the STANDBY POWER switch on the Pilots' Forward Overhead Panel (P5) to
the AUTO position.
3) Align the Air Data Inertial Reference System (ADIRS). Do this task: Air Data Inertial
Reference System - Alignment from the ISDU, TASK 34-21-00-820-802 or Air Data
Inertial Reference System - Alignment from the FMC CDU,
TASK 34-21-00-820-801.
4) Make sure that all the instrument switches, on the P5 Forward Overhead Panel, are
in the NORMAL position.
5) Make sure that the airplane is in the ground mode.
22-11-34
6) Make sure that the autopilot is disengaged.
7) Make sure that the Inertial Reference System (IRS) ground speed is less than 20
knots or the Flight Management Computer (FMC) ground speed is less than 20
knots.
(b) Push the INIT REF key on the Flight Management Computer System (FMCS) Control
Display Unit (CDU).
(c) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) adjacent to <INDEX.
(d) Push the LSK adjacent to MAINT>.
(e) Push the LSK adjacent to <DFCS.
NOTE: The DFCS self-test will start and will run for a few seconds.
(f) Push the LSK adjacent to <LRU REPLACEMENT TESTS.
(g) Push the LSK adjacent to <CHANNEL A AND B.
(h) Push the LSK adjacent to <MCP .
(i) Do the preparation steps as shown on the CDU display.
(j) Push the LSK adjacent to <CONTINUE.
NOTE: The A/P (Autopilot) disengage aural warning may sound during the test. This
warning does not affect performance and test result. Push the captain or first
officer autopilot disengage switch to clear the A/P disengage aural warning after
the test is completed.
(k) Make sure that the test is completed with no failure.
NOTE: If a failure occurs, the TEST FAILED indication will show on the CDU display.
(l) If a failure occurs, do these steps:
1) Look at the CDU display and make a note of the channel and the LRU that caused
the failure.
NOTE: Before you replace a defective component, do the Built-In-Test Equipment
(BITE) on the system (if it is applicable) and on the FMS interface
subsystems that have the defective component.
2) Replace the LRU that had the failure.
3) Do the LRU REPLACEMENT TEST for DFCS MCP again.
(m) Make sure that the CDU display shows TEST PASSED.
1) Make sure that the INTERACTIVE TEST PASSED shows on the CDU display at the
end of each interactive test.
NOTE: Some tests are too fast to see. The tests passed unless TEST FAILED
shows on the CDU display.
SUBTASK 22-11-34-740-002

22-11-34-900-801DFCS Mode Control Panel Knobs - ReplacementPDF p. 550
