4. DFCS Mode Control Panel Knobs - Replacement
(Figure 203)
A. General
(1) This task includes the steps to replace the left course indicator control knob, right course
indicator control knob, speed indicator control knob, heading indicator control knob, or indicator
control altitude knob.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Prepare for the Replacement
SUBTASK 22-11-34-040-001
(1) Do this task: DFCS Mode Control Panel - Deactivation, TASK 22-11-34-040-801.
D. DFCS Mode Control Panel Knobs Replacement
SUBTASK 22-11-34-960-001
(1) Replace the left course indicator control knob [1].
(a) Loosen the setscrews [2] on the left course indicator control knob [1].
(b) Remove the left course indicator control knob [1] by pulling on it.
(c) Install the new left course indicator control knob [1] onto the indicator shaft.
(d) Make sure that there is clearance of 0.03 ±0.01 in. (0.76 ±0.25 mm) between the bottom
face of the knob and the top face of the control panel.
(e) Install the setscrews [2] in the left course indicator control knob [1].
1) Tighten the screws to 3.5 in-lb (0.4 N·m) - 4.25 in-lb (0.48 N·m).
SUBTASK 22-11-34-960-002
(2) Replace the speed indicator control knob [3].
(a) Loosen the setscrews [4] on the speed indicator control knob [3].
(b) Remove the speed indicator control knob [3] by pulling on it.
(c) Install the new speed indicator control knob [3] onto the indicator shaft.
(d) Make sure that there is clearance of 0.03 ±0.01 in. (0.76 ±0.25 mm) between the bottom
face of the knob and the top face of the control panel.
(e) Install the setscrews [4] in the speed indicator control knob [3].
1) Tighten the screws to 3.5 in-lb (0.4 N·m) - 4.25 in-lb (0.48 N·m).
SUBTASK 22-11-34-960-003
(3) Replace the heading indicator control knob [5].
(a) Loosen the setscrews [6] on the heading indicator control knob [5].
(b) Remove the heading indicator control knob [5] by pulling on it.
(c) Install the new heading indicator control knob [5] onto the indicator shaft.
(d) Make sure that there is clearance of 0.03 ±0.01 in. (0.76 ±0.25 mm) between the bottom
face of the knob and the top face of the bank limit knob.
(e) Install the setscrews [6] in the heading indicator control knob [5].
22-11-34
1) Tighten the screws to 3.5 in-lb (0.4 N·m) - 4.25 in-lb (0.48 N·m).

SUBTASK 22-11-39-860-002
(2) Make sure that the engines are not in operation.
22-11-39
SUBTASK 22-11-39-860-003
(3) Put the thrust lever in a position so that you can get access to the switch [6].
SUBTASK 22-11-39-010-001
(4) Remove the applicable cover [4] or [5] from the left or right thrust lever.
(a) Remove the screws [7] from the applicable cover.
SUBTASK 22-11-39-020-001
(5) Do these steps to remove the switch [6]:
(a) Remove the lacing tape that attaches the TOGA switch wire bundle.
(b) Remove the screws [8] which attach the switch holder [3] and the switch [6] to the thrust
lever.
(c) Remove the hex nut [1], and the lockwasher [2] from the switch [6].
(d) Remove the switch [6] from the switch holder [3].
(e) Remove the heat shrinkable RT-876 sleeve, G01148 or Versafit heat shrink tubing,
G51210 from the switch terminals.
(f) Use the soldering gun, STD-3905 to melt the solder on the switch terminals to release the
wires from the switch [6].
1) Make a record of the wire positions and the locations of the switch terminals.
(g) Remove the switch [6].

(2) Do these steps to install the switch [6]:
(a) Put the wire for each terminal of the switch [6] through a heat shrinkable RT-876 sleeve,
G01148, or Versafit heat shrink tubing, G51210.
(b) Use the soldering gun, STD-3905, to solder the wires to the new TOGA switch terminals.
(c) Push the heat shrinkable RT-876 sleeve, G01148, or Versafit heat shrink tubing, G51210,
on all of the switch terminals.
(d) Use the heat 180 deg. F (82 deg. C) maximum output temperature heat gun, STD-442, to
blow the hot air on the heat shrinkable RT-876 sleeve, G01148, or Versafit heat shrink
tubing, G51210, to make it smaller and tighter.
(e) Put the switch [6] in the switch holder [3].
1) Install the lockwasher [2] and hex nut [1].
(f) Install the switch [6] and switch holder [3] in the thrust lever with the screws [8].
(g) Use the lacing tape to attach the TOGA switch wire bundle.
(h) Install the applicable cover [4] or cover [5] with the screws [7].

G. Hydraulic Pressure Switch Installation
SUBTASK 22-11-40-020-002
(1) Remove the cover from the hydraulic port.
SUBTASK 22-11-40-420-001
(2) Do these steps to install the hydraulic pressure switch [1]:
22-11-40
(a) Apply a layer of the MCS 352B fluid, D00054, or hydraulic fluid, D00153, to the new
packing [2].
(b) Apply a layer of the hydraulic MCS 352B fluid, D00054, or hydraulic fluid, D00153, to the
threads of the hydraulic pressure switch [1].
(c) Install the packing [2] on the hydraulic pressure switch [1].
(d) Install the hydraulic pressure switch [1].
(e) Tighten the aileron hydraulic pressure switch [1] to 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m) - 115 in-lb
(13.0 N·m).
(f) Tighten the elevator hydraulic pressure switch [1] to 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m) - 120 in-lb
(13.6 N·m).
(g) Install MS20995NC32 lockwire, G01912, on the hydraulic pressure switch [1] per double
twist method (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
(h) Examine the connector for corrosion (SWPM 20-60-03).
1) If there is corrosion, refer to SWPM 20-60-03 to correct the problem.
(i) Apply the D5026NS or ZC-026 compound, G50171, to the connector (SWPM 20-60-03).
(j) Connect the electrical connector to the hydraulic pressure switch [1].

I. Installation Test
SUBTASK 22-11-40-740-001
(1) Do this BITE test:
(a) Push the INIT REF key on the Control Display Unit (CDU) keyboard.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) that is adjacent to each selection:
NOTE: If the CONTINUE shows during the BITE test, then do the instructions that show
on the CDU display before you push the LSK that is adjacent to CONTINUE. Use
the NEXT PAGE or PREV PAGE key to change page if it is necessary.
1) INDEX
2) MAINT
3) DFCS
4) EXTENDED MAINTENANCE
5) BITE LIBRARY TEST
22-11-40
6) Make a selection for the applicable channel:
NOTE: The A/P aileron actuator A and A/P elevator actuator A have interfaces with
the Flight Control Computer A (FCC-A) (Channel A). The A/P aileron
actuator B and A/P elevator actuator B have interfaces with the Flight
Control Computer B (FCC-B) (Channel B).
7) CHANNEL A
8) CHANNEL B
9) CHANNEL A AND B
10) RUN SELECT LIBRARY TESTS
11) 30 ELEV CONTROL or 31 AIL CONTROL
NOTE: The 30 ELEV CONTROL is for the A/P elevator actuators and 31 AIL
CONTROL is for the A/P aileron actuators.
12) EXECUTE
13) Do the instructions that show on the CDU to complete the test.
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020; AIRPLANES WITH HONEYWELL FCC HARDWARE INSTALLED
NOTE: If the test of the library 60.30 (30 ELEVATOR) shows “TEST FAILED” at
step S053, but the fail values on the CDU are between the expanded limits
(-2.88, -2.10) and the remainder of the test passes, then the full test is a
TEST PASS.
NOTE: If the system continues to fail with expanded limits, it is necessary to check
the friction in the elevator cable system, including all pulleys. Also it is
necessary to check the spring force of the springs within the feel of
centering (TASK 27-31-64-400-802).
RYR ALL
14) Make sure that the test is completed with no failure or the "TEST FAILED" message
does not show.
SUBTASK 22-11-40-210-001
(2) Visually examine the hydraulic pressure switch.
(a) Make sure that there is no hydraulic leak.

SUBTASK 22-11-41-020-001
(2) Do these steps to remove the switch [1]:
(a) At the center of the control wheel, remove the screws [4] on the medallion and approach
plate [5].
NOTE: The medallion is part of the approach plate.
1) Remove the medallion and approach plate [5].
(b) Use the extraction insertion/extraction tool, COM-3934 to disconnect the eight wires on
the autopilot disengage switch side of the wire connectors in the center of the control
wheel.
(c) Attach one end of the threading wire around the eight wires that connect to the switch [1].
NOTE: For the threading wire, you can use a 30-inch (76.2 centimeters) length of
standard wire (No. 18 to 20 gage, without insulation).
(d) Remove the screw [3] on the outboard horn of the control wheel.
(e) Remove the switch plate [2].
(f) Pull the switch [1] and its eight wires through the control wheel horn slowly until the
threading wire shows.
NOTE: Pull until approximately 3 inches (76.2 millimeters) of the threading wire comes
out of the control wheel horn. Do not remove the threading wire. It is necessary
to use this threading wire for the installation.
(g) Remove the eight wires from the threading wire.

disengage the autopilot.
22-11-41
1) Make sure that the A/P warning lights flash.
2) Make sure that the A/P disengage warning sound comes on.
3) Push the captain or first officer autopilot disengage switch again.
a) Make sure that the A/P warning lights go off.
b) Make sure that there is no A/P disengage warning sound.

22-11-81
SUBTASK 22-11-81-860-003
(7) Make sure that the trailing edge flaps are in the UP position.
SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-002
(8) Engage the autopilot A in the CWS mode.
SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-003
(9) Pull the control column to the aft position approximately 2 inches (51 mm). Release the control
column. To prevent a camout of the control column, keep the force on the control column below
15 pounds.
NOTE: These conditions can cause the STAB OUT OF TRIM light to come on during the test:
1. Camout of the control column.
2. STAB NOSE UP or STAB NOSE DOWN limit switches are engaged.
3. The Stab Trim Motor is bad and did not operate at the correct rate.
SUBTASK 22-11-81-210-001
(10) Make sure that these occur:
(a) The stabilizer starts to move.
(b) The stabilizer leading edge moves down at a rate of 3 units in 30 to 40 seconds.
SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-004

SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-005

SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-006

SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-007
(14) Engage the autopilot B in the CWS mode.
SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-008
(15) Push the control column to the fwd position approximately 2 inches (51 mm). Release the
control column. To prevent a camout of the control column, keep the force on the control
column below 15 pounds.
NOTE: These conditions can cause the STAB OUT OF TRIM light to come on during the test:
1. Camout of the control column.
2. STAB NOSE UP or STAB NOSE DOWN limit switches are engaged.
3. The Stab Trim Motor is bad and did not operate at the correct rate.
SUBTASK 22-11-81-210-002
(16) Make sure that these occur:
(a) The stabilizer starts to move.
(b) The stabilizer leading edge moves up at a rate of 1 unit in 4 to 7 seconds.
SUBTASK 22-11-81-980-009
(17) Disengage the autopilot B.

NOTE: The B dimension is measured between the center of the upper and lower Gimbal pins

airplane with a headset and the other person in the flight compartment
(a) Make sure that the MAIN STABILIZER TRIM CUTOUT switch, on the control stand, is in

STAY AWAY FROM THE STABILIZER TRIM WHEEL ON THE CONTROL
STAND IF YOU USE THE STABILIZER TRIM SWITCH ON THE CONTROL
WHEEL. THE STABILIZER TRIM SWITCH CAN CAUSE THE STABILIZER
TRIM WHEEL TO TURN QUICKLY . INJURY TO PERSONS CAN OCCUR IF
YOU TOUCH IT WHEN IT TURNS.

NOTE: This B dimension is equivalent to the horizontal stabilizer at 4 units of trim (zero
degrees).

SHARP EDGES THAT CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE WIRE BUNDLE
INSULATION.

to the transducer.

(13) Remove the lockwire from the locknut [8], locknut [18], locktab [17], and adjusting nut [12] on
the captain's (or first officer's) pitch force transducer [1].
SUBTASK 22-11-92-020-008
(14) Loosen the locknut [8] so that the adjusting nut [12] can be turned with fingers.
SUBTASK 22-11-92-820-002
(15) Adjust the pitch force transducer [1] so the adjustable rod end [7] can be installed.
NOTE: For coarse adjustment, turn the rod end. For a fine adjustment, turn the adjusting nut.
SUBTASK 22-11-92-211-001
(16) Make sure that the null voltage shown on the CDU is within 0.040 VAC of the value you
recorded earlier.
SUBTASK 22-11-92-020-009
(17) Remove the bolt [16], washer [14], and nut [13].
NOTE: This bolt is removed as part of the rigging procedure then reinstalled in subsequent
operations.
SUBTASK 22-11-92-480-003
(18) Install the rig pin E-2 (captain's) or the rig pin E-3 (first officer's) through the hole where the
bolt [16] was removed.
SUBTASK 22-11-92-210-001
(19) Make sure that the rig pin E-2 (captain's) or the rig pin E-3 (first officer's), from the rigging pin
kit, SPL-1585, can be freely inserted.
SUBTASK 22-11-92-420-001
(20) Do these steps to install the adjustable rod end [7] of the pitch force transducer [1]:
(a) Install the bolt [6], washer [10], and nut [11].

(c) Tighten the locknut [8] on the adjusting nut [12] for the pitch force transducer [1].
(d) Install MS20995NC20 lockwire, G02166, for the locknut [8], locknut [18], locktab [17],
adjusting nut [12] on the pitch force transducer [1] (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
22-11-92
SUBTASK 22-11-92-080-002

assemblies.

SUBTASK 22-11-92-420-007

SUBTASK 22-11-92-420-008
(24) Install the mass balance weight [4] with the bolts [2] and washers [3].
(a) Tighten the bolts [2].
(b) Install the MS20995NC32 lockwire, G50347, on the bolts [2] (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
SUBTASK 22-11-92-080-003

SUBTASK 22-11-92-710-001
(26) Do this check on the pitch force transducer [1]:
(a) Move the control columns lightly until the system is in the center.
(b) Make sure that the A and B voltages on the CDU display are less than ±0.20 VAC.
(c) Make sure that the difference between the A and B voltages is less than ±0.20 VAC.
1) If the voltage is larger than ±0.20 VAC, do a check on the control quadrant and
bearings of the structure.
2) If they are satisfactory, do a check of the cable tension for elevator control cables
EA and EB (TASK 27-31-00-820-801).

1) RUD CMD RIGHT

G. Standby Rudder PCU Solenoid and Electrohydraulic Servo Valves Installation
SUBTASK 22-23-11-020-003
(1) Do these steps to install the solenoid valve [3]:
(a) Remove the plugs on the standby rudder PCU ports.
(b) Lubricate the o-ring [7] with hydraulic fluid, D00153.
(c) Install the new o-ring [7].
22-23-11
(d) Install the gasket plate [9].
(e) Install the solenoid valve [3].
(f) Lightly coat the threads of the bolts [4] with grease, D50106, BATCO X8401-1 grease,
D50105, or BATCO X8401-2 grease, D00571.
(g) Install the bolts [4] that attach the solenoid valve [3] to the standby rudder PCU.
1) Tighten the bolts [4] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) - 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m).
(h) Apply a fillet seal to the interface between the bolts [4] and solenoid valve [3], do the
following steps:
1) Use solvent, B50281, to clean the area and let the area dry.
2) Apply a bead of PR-1440 B-2 sealant, A50527, or PR-1440 B-1/2 sealant, A50387,
around the head of each of the bolts [4] and let a sealant cure.
3) Use Akzo Nobel 683-3-2 coating, C00012, or Akzo Nobel 683-3-20 coating,
C50238, to apply a top coating on the sealant.
(i) Install a MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, on the bolts [4].
(j) Apply a fillet seal to the interface between the solenoid valve [3] and standby rudder
PCU, do the following steps:
1) Use technical grade methyl ethyl ketone, B50046, to clean the area and let the area
dry.
2) Apply a bead of sealant, A50419, or sealant, A50110, around the interface between
the solenoid valve [3] and gasket plate [9] and between the gasket plate [9] and
standby rudder PCU.
SUBTASK 22-23-11-020-004
(2) Do these steps to install the electrohydraulic servo valve [5]:
(a) Remove the plugs on the standby rudder PCU ports.
(b) Lubricate the o-ring [7] with hydraulic fluid, D00153.
(c) Install the new o-ring [7].
(d) Install the gasket plate [8].
(e) Install the electrohydraulic servo valve [5].
(f) Lightly coat the threads of the bolts [6] with grease, D50106, BATCO X8401-1 grease,
D50105, or BATCO X8401-2 grease, D00571.
(g) Install the bolts [6] that attach the electrohydraulic servo valve [5] to the standby rudder
PCU.
1) Tighten the bolts [6] to 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m) - 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m).
(h) Apply a fillet seal to the interface between the bolts [6] and electrohydraulic servo
valve [5], do the following steps:
1) Use solvent, B50281, to clean the area and let the area dry.
2) Apply a bead of PR-1440 B-2 sealant, A50527, or PR-1440 B-1/2 sealant, A50387,
around the head of each of the bolts [6] and let a sealant cure.
3) Use Akzo Nobel 683-3-2 coating, C00012, or Akzo Nobel 683-3-20 coating,
C50238, to apply a top coating on the sealant.
(i) Install a MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048, on the bolts [6].
(j) Apply a fillet seal to the interface between the electrohydraulic servo valve [5] and
standby rudder PCU, do the following steps:
22-23-11
1) Use technical grade methyl ethyl ketone, B50046, to clean the area and let the area
dry.
2) Apply a bead of sealant, A50419, or sealant, A50110, around the interface between
the electrohydraulic servo valve [5] and gasket plate [8] and between the gasket
plate [8] and standby rudder PCU.

22-23-21
E. Procedure
SUBTASK 22-23-21-020-001
(1) Do these steps to remove the solenoid valve [4]:
(a) Remove the bolts [5 ] that attach the solenoid valve [4] to the main rudder PCU.
(b) Remove the solenoid valve [4].
(c) Remove the gasket plate [3].
1) If the seals on the gasket plate [3] are in bad condition, discard the gasket plate [3].
(d) Remove and discard the o-ring [8].
(e) Put plugs on the main rudder PCU ports to avoid hydraulic fluid contamination.
SUBTASK 22-23-21-020-002
(2) Do these steps to remove the electrohydraulic servo valve [6]:
(a) Remove the bolts [7] that attach the electrohydraulic servo valve [6] to the main rudder
PCU.
(b) Remove the electrohydraulic servo valve [6].
(c) Remove the gasket plate [9].
1) If the seals on the gasket plate [9] are in bad condition, discard the gasket plate [9].
(d) Remove and discard the o-ring [8].
(e) Put plugs on the main rudder PCU ports to avoid hydraulic fluid contamination.

G. Main Rudder PCU Solenoid and Electrohydraulic Servo Valves Installation
SUBTASK 22-23-21-020-003
(1) Do these steps to install the solenoid valve [4]:
(a) Remove the plugs on the main rudder PCU ports.
(b) Lubricate the o-ring [8] with hydraulic fluid, D00153.
(c) Install the new o-ring [8].
(d) Install the gasket plate [3].
(e) Install the solenoid valve [4].
(f) Install the bolts [5 ] that attach the solenoid valve [4] to the main rudder PCU.
(g) Tighten the bolts [5 ] to 54 in-lb (6.1 N·m) - 72 in-lb (8.1 N·m).
(h) Install the MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048 on the bolts [5 ].
SUBTASK 22-23-21-020-004
(2) Do these steps to install the electrohydraulic servo valve [6]:
(a) Remove the plugs on the main rudder PCU ports.
(b) Lubricate the o-ring [8] with hydraulic fluid, D00153.
(c) Install new o-ring [8].
(d) Install the gasket plate [9].
22-23-21
(e) Install the electrohydraulic servo valve [6].
(f) Install the bolts [7] that attach the electrohydraulic servo valve [6] to the main rudder
PCU.
(g) Tighten the bolts [7] to 54 in-lb (6.1 N·m) - 72 in-lb (8.1 N·m).
(h) Install the MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048 on the bolts [7].

2. Autothrottle System - Operational Test
A. General
(1) The operational test uses autothrottle Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) to do a fast check of the
system. Use the Flight Management Computer (FMC) Control Display Unit (CDU) on the P9
panel to do the BITE procedure.
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020
NOTE: The A/T - P/RST light will stay on during Autothrottle BITE testing.

3. Autothrottle System - System Test
A. General
(1) The system test uses autothrottle BITE to make sure that the system operates correctly. Use
the flight management computer control display unit (FMC CDU) on the P9 panel to do the
BITE procedures.
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020
NOTE: The A/T - P/RST light will stay on during A/T BITE testing.
22-31-00
RYR 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187,
189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR
006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020 WITH COLLINS E-FCC SOFTWARE P8.0 OR WITH COLLINS E-FCC SOFTWARE
P9.0 OR WITH COLLINS E-FCC SOFTWARE P11.1
NOTE: The A/T - P/RST and the A/P - P/RST light will stay on during Autothrottle BITE testing.

officer's autothrottle warning lights flash.

a) Make sure that the autothrottle warning lights go out.

a) Make sure that the captain's and first officer's autothrottle warning lights flash.

a) Make sure that the autothrottle warning lights go out.

a) Make sure that the autothrottle warning lights go out.

K. Torque Test
SUBTASK 22-31-00-860-013
(1) Do these steps for the torque test:
(a) Push the LSK adjacent to THROTTLE TESTS.
22-31-00
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT ENGINE START LEVERS ARE IN THE CUTOFF
POSITION. IF THE ENGINE START LEVERS ARE NOT IN THE CUTOFF
POSITION, THE ENGINE START SEQUENCE CAN OCCUR. THIS CAN
CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(b) Do the instructions on the CDU.
(c) Push the LSK adjacent to CONTINUE.
SUBTASK 22-31-00-740-007
(2) Push the LSK adjacent to TORQUE TEST .
(a) Do the instructions on the CDU.
(b) Push the LSK adjacent to CONTINUE.
(c) Make sure that the torque does not exceed 11 in-oz while the thrust levers are moving.
(d) Make sure that the torque does not exceed 17 in-oz while motors are driving the thrust
levers against the aft stop.
(e) Make sure that the torque on the CDU is within approximately +/- 1.5 in-oz of stable
values during these phases of the test:
1) Start of test, no thrust levers movement
2) Thrust levers moving toward forward stop
3) Thrust levers moving toward aft stop.
NOTE: 1) It is important that the thrust levers reach steady state torques and stay
within +/- 1.5 in-oz of stable values at each phase, this is testing for smooth
lever movement free of restrictions.
2) When the thrust levers get to their limits of travel the torque may increase
by approximately 3.0 to 6.0 in-oz.
3) The CDU has a 2 second screen update rate, the torque shown may not
be consistent with a stable value during phase changes due to this delay.
4) It is not expected that Throttle-1 and Throttle-2 torque reading to be the
same value.
(f) Push the LSK adjacent to INDEX.
1) Make sure that the A/T BITE TEST INTERACTIVE THROTTLE TESTS page shows
on the CDU.
(g) Push the LSK adjacent to INDEX again.
(h) Do the instructions on the CDU.
(i) Push the LSK adjacent to CONTINUE.
(j) Push the LSK adjacent to INDEX.
(k) Push the LSK adjacent to INDEX again.
(l) Make sure that the MAIN BITE INDEX page shows on the CDU.

2. Autothrottle Brake Assembly - Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) There are two autothrottle brake assemblies on an airplane.
(2) The autothrottle brake assemblies are installed on the autothrottle assembly. The autothrottle
assembly is located inside the forward access door.
B. References
Reference Title
22-31-91-020-801 Autothrottle Servo Motor and Gearbox Removal (P/B 401)
76-11-05-000-801-F00 Thrust Lever Angle Resolver and Autothrottle Brake Assembly
Removal (P/B 401)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
112 Area Forward of Nose Landing Gear Wheel Well
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Prepare for the Removal
SUBTASK 22-31-81-000-001
(1) Remove the associated autothrottle servo motor and gearbox. To remove it, do this task:
Autothrottle Servo Motor and Gearbox Removal, TASK 22-31-91-020-801.
SUBTASK 22-31-81-010-001
(2) Remove the associated autothrottle resolver. To remove it, do this task: Thrust Lever Angle
Resolver and Autothrottle Brake Assembly Removal, TASK 76-11-05-000-801-F00.
E. Autothrottle Brake Assembly Removal
SUBTASK 22-31-81-020-001
(1) Do these steps to disconnect the thrust lever connecting rod [6]:
(a) Remove the cotter pin [3].
(b) Remove the bolt [1], washer [5], and nut [4] that holds the thrust lever connecting rod [6]
to the brake assembly [2].
SUBTASK 22-31-81-020-002
(2) Remove the bolt [10], washer [9], and nut [8] to disconnect the control rod [11].
SUBTASK 22-31-81-010-002
(3) Slide the brake assembly [2] off the shaft [7].

2. Autothrottle Servo Motor and Gearbox Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) The autothrottle servo motor (referred to as the servo motor) is installed on the gearbox of the
autothrottle assembly.
(2) The gearbox is installed on the autothrottle assembly.
(3) The servo motor and the gearbox can also be removed and installed as a unit. To remove both
as a unit, do not remove the servo motor from the gearbox.
B. References
Reference Title
53-14-01-020-801 Nose Wheel Well Access Panels - Removal (P/B 401)

(a) Remove the bolts [8], nuts [10], countersunk washers [12], and flat washers [9] that hold

F . Gearbox Installation
SUBTASK 22-31-91-640-001
(1) Apply grease, D00633, on all mating surfaces before you assemble the mechanism.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-001
(2) Align the gearbox spline on the spline shaft and set the gearbox [1] against the support frame.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-002
(3) Install the bolts [5] and washers [11] on the gearbox [1].
22-31-91
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-003
(4) Align the spline teeth of the ramp plug [2] with the spline teeth of the shaft so that the adjacent
ramp faces make contact with each other.
NOTE: A properly installed ramp plug will have the end of the ramp plug approximately even
with the spline relief on the gearbox worm gear. If the end of the ramp plug is too high
or below the spline relief, adjust it as follows: remove the ramp plug, turn it, and
engage the ramp plug spline again.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-004
(5) Install the screw [4] with the washer [3] through the ramp plug [2]:
(a) Tighten the screw [4] to 25 in-lb (2.8 N·m) - 35 in-lb (4.0 N·m).
G. Autothrottle Servo Motor Installation
SUBTASK 22-31-91-640-002
(1) Apply grease, D00633, on all mating surfaces before you assemble the mechanism.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-005
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE SERVO MOTOR CAREFULLY . IF YOU
DO NOT , DAMAGE TO THE SPLINE SHAFT ON THE SERVO MOTOR CAN
OCCUR.
(2) Carefully, attach the autothrottle servo motor [6] onto the gearbox [1].
(a) Manually turn the autothrottle servo motor [6] shaft spline to align with the grooves in the
gearbox [1] (Figure 402).
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-009
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE SERVO MOTOR CAREFULLY . IF YOU
DO NOT , DAMAGE TO THE SPLINE SHAFT ON THE SERVO MOTOR CAN
OCCUR.
(3) Carefully, attach the autothrottle servo motor [13] onto the gearbox [1].
(a) Manually turn the autothrottle servo motor [13] shaft spline to align with the grooves in the
gearbox [1] (Figure 402).
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-007
(4) Install the bolts [8], countersunk washers [12], flat washers [9], and nuts [10] that hold the left
hand servo motor [6] to the gearbox [1].
(a) Before you tighten the bolts [8], make sure that there is no clearance between the servo
motor [6] front plate and the gearbox [1] mounting lugs (Figure 402).
SUBTASK 22-31-91-420-008
(5) Install the bolts [14], countersunk washers [15], flat washers [16], and nuts [17] that hold the
right hand servo motor [13] and guard [18] to the gearbox [1].
(a) Before you tighten the bolts [14], make sure that there is no clearance between the servo

H. Autothrottle Servo Motor Installation Test
SUBTASK 22-31-91-860-006
(1) Make sure that the IRS-L and IRS-R mode select switches, on the P5 overhead panel, are not
in the OFF position.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-740-001
(2) Do this Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) test:
(a) Push the INIT REF key on the Flight Management Computer (FMC) Control Display Unit
(CDU) keyboard.
(b) Push the Line Select Key (LSK) adjacent to INDEX.
(c) Push the LSK adjacent to MAINT .
(d) Push the LSK adjacent to A/T .
1) Make sure that the autothrottle warning lights on the autoflight status annunciators
(P1 and P3 panels) come on continuously.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT ENGINE START LEVERS ARE IN THE CUTOFF
POSITION. IF THE ENGINE START LEVERS ARE NOT IN THE CUTOFF
POSITION, THE ENGINE START SEQUENCE CAN OCCUR. IF YOU DO
NOT OBEY INSTRUCTIONS, DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT AND INJURY TO
PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
(e) Push the LSK adjacent to CURRENT STATUS.
(f) Do the instructions on the CDU.
(g) Push the LSK adjacent to CONTINUE.
1) Make sure that TEST IN PROGRESS shows on the CDU.
2) Make sure that the A/T switch, on the P7 panel, automatically goes back to OFF .
3) Make sure that NO FAULTS shows on the CDU when the test is completed.
(h) Push the LSK adjacent to INDEX.
(i) Do the instructions on the CDU.
22-31-91
(j) Push the LSK adjacent to CONTINUE.
1) Make sure that A/T BITE TEST index shows.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-700-001
(3) Do the Servo motor test (TASK 22-31-00-710-802).
(a) Push the LSK adjacent to INDEX.
(b) Push the INIT REF key on the FMC CDU keyboard.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-860-012
(4) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1
Row Col Number Name
D 13 C00120 WEATHER RADAR RT
SUBTASK 22-31-91-860-007
(5) If Inertial Reference System (IRS) outputs are no longer necessary, set the IRS-L and IRS-R
mode select switches, on the P5 overhead panel, to the OFF position.
SUBTASK 22-31-91-860-011
(6) Remove the DO-NOT-OPERATE tags on the thrust levers.
